“That’s it, ‘Will an’ the children,’ ” he repeated.
But there was no bitterness in his voice. He had long known his mother’s ambition for the younger boy, but the thought of it no longer rankled. Nothing mattered any more. Not even that.
“I know, ma, what you’ve ben plannin’ for Will﻿—keepin’ him in school to make a bookkeeper out of him. But it ain’t no use, I’ve quit. He’s got to go to work.”

===Chinese===
“就是这话，‘威尔和孩子们’，”他重复道。
可他的话音里没有一点苦味。母亲对小儿子的指望，他早就知道，可这念头再也不能刺痛他了。什么都不打紧了。连这个也不打紧。
“我知道，妈，你给威尔盘算的是什么——供他念书，把他造就成一个账房先生。可没有用了，我不干了。他得去做工。”

===Original===
“An’ after I have brung you up the way I have,” she wept, starting to cover her head with the apron and changing her mind.
“You never brung me up,” he answered with sad kindliness. “I brung myself up, ma, an’ I brung up Will. He’s bigger’n me, an’ heavier, an’ taller. When I was a kid, I reckon I didn’t git enough to eat. When he come along an’ was a kid, I was workin’ an’ earnin’ grub for him too. But that’s done with. Will can go to work, same as me, or he can go to hell, I don’t care which. I’m tired. I’m goin’ now. Ain’t you goin’ to say goodbye?”
She made no reply. The apron had gone over her head again, and she was crying. He paused a moment in the doorway.

===Chinese===
“我可是这么把你拉扯大的，”她哭道，抬手正要用围裙蒙头，又变了主意。
“你从没拉扯过我，”他带着凄凉的温厚答道。“是我自己把自己拉扯大的，妈，我还拉扯大了威尔。他比我壮，比我重，比我高。我做娃娃那阵，我算着是没吃饱过的。轮到他是娃娃那阵，我在做工，也在替他挣嚼谷。可如今算完了。威尔可以去做工，跟我一样；也可以下地狱去，随他的便。我累了。我走了。你不道个别么？”
她没有回答。围裙又蒙上了头，她哭着。他在门口停了停。

===Original===
“I’m sure I done the best I knew how,” she was sobbing.
He passed out of the house and down the street. A wan delight came into his face at the sight of the lone tree. “Jes’ ain’t goin’ to do nothin’,” he said to himself, half aloud, in a crooning tone. He glanced wistfully up at the sky, but the bright sun dazzled and blinded him.
It was a long walk he took, and he did not walk fast. It took him past the jute-mill. The muffled roar of the loom room came to his ears, and he smiled. It was a gentle, placid smile. He hated no one, not even the pounding, shrieking machines. There was no bitterness in him, nothing but an inordinate hunger for rest.

===Chinese===
“我真的尽了最大的力了，”她呜咽着。
他出了门，沿街走去。望见那棵孤零零的树，他脸上浮起一抹淡淡的欢喜。“就是什么都不干，”他自言自语，半出着声，哼哄似的调子。他怅然抬头望天，可明亮的太阳晃得他眼前发黑。
他走了长长的一段路，走得不快。路把他带过了黄麻厂。织布车间那闷住的咆哮传进他耳朵，他微笑了。那是一抹温良、平和的微笑。他不恨任何人，连那些撞击、尖啸的机器也不恨。他心里没有苦味，只有一份对歇息的、没有节制的饥饿。

===Original===
The houses and factories thinned out and the open spaces increased as he approached the country. At last the city was behind him, and he was walking down a leafy lane beside the railroad track. He did not walk like a man. He did not look like a man. He was a travesty of the human. It was a twisted and stunted and nameless piece of life that shambled like a sickly ape, arms loose-hanging, stoop-shouldered, narrow-chested, grotesque and terrible.
He passed by a small railroad station and lay down in the grass under a tree. All afternoon he lay there. Sometimes he dozed, with muscles that twitched in his sleep. When awake, he lay without movement, watching the birds or looking up at the sky through the branches of the tree above him. Once or twice he laughed aloud, but without relevance to anything he had seen or felt.
After twilight had gone, in the first darkness of the night, a freight train rumbled into the station. When the engine was switching cars on to the sidetrack, Johnny crept along the side of the train. He pulled open the side-door of an empty boxcar and awkwardly and laboriously climbed in. He closed the door. The engine whistled. Johnny was lying down, and in the darkness he smiled.

===Chinese===
走近乡下，房屋和厂房渐渐稀了，空地渐渐多了。城市终于落在了身后，他正顺着铁路旁一条绿荫小路走下去。他走路不像个人。他模样也不像个人。他是人的一个拙劣戏仿。那是一段扭曲的、发育不良的、没有名目的生命，拖着脚步蹭着走，像一头病猿，两臂松垂，佝肩缩胸，怪诞而可怕。
他走过一座小车站，在一棵树下的草地上躺下来。一整个下午他都躺在那儿。有时打打盹，睡着了肌肉还在抽动。醒着的时候，他一动不动地躺着，看鸟，或者透过头顶的树枝望天。有一两回他笑出了声，却同他看见的、感到的任何东西都不相干。
暮色散尽，入夜最初的黑暗里，一列货车隆隆驶进车站。机车正把车厢往侧线上调，约翰尼顺着列车一侧悄悄蹭过去。他拉开一节空棚车的侧门，笨手笨脚、费力地爬了进去。他关上门。机车鸣了汽笛。约翰尼已经躺下，黑暗里，他笑了。

## A Wicked Woman / 一个坏女人

===Original===
It was because she had broken with Billy that Loretta had come visiting to Santa Clara. Billy could not understand. His sister had reported that he had walked the floor and cried all night. Loretta had not slept all night either, while she had wept most of the night. Daisy knew this, because it was in her arms that the weeping had been done. And Daisy’s husband, Captain Kitt, knew, too. The tears of Loretta, and the comforting by Daisy, had lost him some sleep.
Now Captain Kitt did not like to lose sleep. Neither did he want Loretta to marry Billy﻿—nor anybody else. It was Captain Kitt’s belief that Daisy needed the help of her younger sister in the household. But he did not say this aloud. Instead, he always insisted that Loretta was too young to think of marriage. So it was Captain Kitt’s idea that Loretta should be packed off on a visit to Mrs. Hemingway. There wouldn’t be any Billy there.
Before Loretta had been at Santa Clara a week, she was convinced that Captain Kitt’s idea was a good one. In the first place, though Billy wouldn’t believe it, she did not want to marry Billy. And in the second place, though Captain Kitt wouldn’t believe it, she did not want to leave Daisy. By the time Loretta had been at Santa Clara two weeks, she was absolutely certain that she did not want to marry Billy. But she was not so sure about not wanting to leave Daisy. Not that she loved Daisy less, but that she﻿—had doubts.
The day of Loretta’s arrival, a nebulous plan began shaping itself in Mrs. Hemingway’s brain. The second day she remarked to Jack Hemingway, her husband, that Loretta was so innocent a young thing that were it not for her sweet guilelessness she would be positively stupid. In proof of which, Mrs. Hemingway told her husband several things that made him chuckle. By the third day Mrs. Hemingway’s plan had taken recognizable form. Then it was that she composed a letter. On the envelope she wrote: “Mr. Edward Bashford, Athenian Club, San Francisco.”
“Dear Ned,” the letter began. She had once been violently loved by him for three weeks in her premarital days. But she had covenanted herself to Jack Hemingway, who had prior claims, and her heart as well; and Ned Bashford had philosophically not broken his heart over it. He merely added the experience to a large fund of similarly collected data out of which he manufactured philosophy. Artistically and temperamentally he was a Greek﻿—a tired Greek. He was fond of quoting from Nietzsche, in token that he, too, had passed through the long sickness that follows upon the ardent search for truth; that he too had emerged, too experienced, too shrewd, too profound, ever again to be afflicted by the madness of youths in their love of truth. “ ‘To worship appearance,’ ” he often quoted; “ ‘to believe in forms, in tones, in words, in the whole Olympus of appearance!’ ” This particular excerpt he always concluded with, “ ‘Those Greeks were superficial﻿—*out of profundity!*’ ”

===Chinese===
洛蕾塔是因为同比利吹了，才到圣克拉拉来做客的。比利想不通。据他姐姐说，他整夜在地上踱来踱去，哭了一宿。洛蕾塔也一夜没睡，大半夜都在掉泪。这事黛西知道，因为那场哭就是伏在她怀里哭的。黛西的丈夫基特船长也知道。洛蕾塔的眼泪，加上黛西的劝慰，害他损失了不少瞌睡。
基特船长可不爱损失瞌睡。他也不想让洛蕾塔嫁给比利——也别嫁给任何人。基特船长认定，家里那些事，黛西少不了妹妹帮衬。这话他没说出口。他翻来覆去只有一个讲法：洛蕾塔还太年轻，谈婚论嫁早着呢。于是把洛蕾塔打发去海明威太太家做客，就是基特船长的主意。那儿没有比利。
洛蕾塔到圣克拉拉不到一个星期，就认定基特船长的主意不坏。第一，虽说比利不肯信，她本来就不想嫁比利。第二，虽说基特船长不肯信，她也不想离开黛西。到圣克拉拉满两个星期的时候，她已经百分之百地拿准了：自己不想嫁比利。至于想不想离开黛西，她倒不那么拿得准了。倒不是她爱黛西爱得浅了，而是她——心里起了疑。
洛蕾塔到的头一天，一个朦朦胧胧的计划就在海明威太太脑子里起了形。第二天，她跟丈夫杰克·海明威念叨，说洛蕾塔这姑娘天真得可以，要不是那份天真烂漫招人疼，简直就蠢得没法说了。为作证明，海明威太太给丈夫讲了几件事，讲得他直乐。到第三天，海明威太太的计划已经眉目分明。于是她提笔写起一封信来。信封上写的是："爱德华·巴什福德先生，旧金山，雅典人俱乐部。"
信的开头是"亲爱的内德"。想当年婚前，他曾狂热地爱过她三个星期。可她把自己许给了杰克·海明威——此人捷足先登，占了礼数，也占了她的心；内德·巴什福德呢，颇有哲人风度，并未为此伤过心。他只不过把这段经历添进一大堆同类收藏里，用它们酿出哲学来。论气质、论性情，他是个希腊人——一个疲惫的希腊人。他爱引尼采，以标榜自己也曾熬过那苦苦求真之后缠绵不去的沉疴，标榜自己也出了坑——历练太深、心眼太多、城府太沉，再不会染上年轻人那种爱真理爱出的疯病。"'崇拜外表，'"他常引，"'相信形式、声调、言辞，相信整个外表的奥林匹斯！'"这一段引完，他总要缀上一句："'那些希腊人是肤浅的——出自深刻！'"

===Original===
He was a fairly young Greek, jaded and worn. Women were faithless and unveracious, he held﻿—at such times that he had relapses and descended to pessimism from his wonted high philosophical calm. He did not believe in the truth of women; but, faithful to his German master, he did not strip from them the airy gauzes that veiled their untruth. He was content to accept them as appearances and to make the best of it. He was superficial﻿—*out of profundity.*
“Jack says to be sure to say to you, ‘good swimming,’ ” Mrs. Hemingway wrote in her letter; “and also ‘to bring your fishing duds along.’ ” Mrs. Hemingway wrote other things in the letter. She told him that at last she was prepared to exhibit to him an absolutely true, unsullied, and innocent woman. “A more guileless, immaculate bud of womanhood never blushed on the planet,” was one of the several ways in which she phrased the inducement. And to her husband she said triumphantly, “If I don’t marry Ned off this time﻿—” leaving unstated the terrible alternative that she lacked either vocabulary to express or imagination to conceive.
Contrary to all her forebodings, Loretta found that she was not unhappy at Santa Clara. Truly, Billy wrote to her every day, but his letters were less distressing than his presence. Also, the ordeal of being away from Daisy was not so severe as she had expected. For the first time in her life she was not lost in eclipse in the blaze of Daisy’s brilliant and mature personality. Under such favourable circumstances Loretta came rapidly to the front, while Mrs. Hemingway modestly and shamelessly retreated into the background.

===Chinese===
他是个年纪尚轻的希腊人，只是乏了、耗损了。女人不忠而且不真——他持这等论调，专在旧病复发的当口，从惯常那种高迈的哲学宁静里跌下来，落进悲观主义。他不信女人身上有真；不过，忠于他的德国宗师，他也不去剥掉那层遮掩不真的轻薄纱衣。他乐于把女人当作表象接受下来，并且将就着处到最好。他肤浅——肤浅得*出自深刻*。
"杰克说务必要向你转致'游得痛快'，"海明威太太在信里写道，"还要'把你的钓鱼行头一并带上'。"信里还写了些别的。她告诉他，她终于预备停当，要给他看一个绝对真实、纯洁无瑕、天真无邪的女人。"开天辟地以来，地球上红过脸的女人里，再没有比这更不知机心、更冰清玉洁的一朵花苞了"——这是她兜售这桩诱惑的几种说法之一。对她丈夫，她得意洋洋地说："这回我要是还不能把内德的婚事办成——"那可怕的下半句她没有说出口，一来她找不着词汇，二来也缺那份想象力。
与种种预感相反，洛蕾塔发现自己待在圣克拉拉并不难过。诚然，比利天天给她写信，可他的信到底不如他的人在跟前叫人难受。还有，离开黛西的熬煎也不如她料想的那样厉害。平生头一遭，她没有在黛西那光彩照人、成熟圆润的个性烈焰里，被烤得黯然无光。趁着这样有利的形势，洛蕾塔飞快地走到前台来，海明威太太则谦逊地——而且恬不知耻地——退进了幕后。

===Original===
Loretta began to discover that she was not a pale orb shining by reflection. Quite unconsciously she became a small centre of things. When she was at the piano, there was some one to turn the pages for her and to express preferences for certain songs. When she dropped her handkerchief, there was some one to pick it up. And there was some one to accompany her in ramblings and flower gatherings. Also, she learned to cast flies in still pools and below savage riffles, and how not to entangle silk lines and gut-leaders with the shrubbery.
Jack Hemingway did not care to teach beginners, and fished much by himself, or not at all, thus giving Ned Bashford ample time in which to consider Loretta as an appearance. As such, she was all that his philosophy demanded. Her blue eyes had the direct gaze of a boy, and out of his profundity he delighted in them and forbore to shudder at the duplicity his philosophy bade him to believe lurked in their depths. She had the grace of a slender flower, the fragility of colour and line of fine china, in all of which he pleasured greatly, without thought of the Life Force palpitating beneath and in spite of Bernard Shaw﻿—in whom he believed.
Loretta burgeoned. She swiftly developed personality. She discovered a will of her own and wishes of her own that were not everlastingly entwined with the will and the wishes of Daisy. She was petted by Jack Hemingway, spoiled by Alice Hemingway, and devotedly attended by Ned Bashford. They encouraged her whims and laughed at her follies, while she developed the pretty little tyrannies that are latent in all pretty and delicate women. Her environment acted as a soporific upon her ancient desire always to live with Daisy. This desire no longer prodded her as in the days of her companionship with Billy. The more she saw of Billy, the more certain she had been that she could not live away from Daisy. The more she saw of Ned Bashford, the more she forgot her pressing need of Daisy.

===Chinese===
洛蕾塔开始发现，自己并不是一颗靠反光发亮的暗淡星球。不知不觉间，她成了一个小小的中心。她坐到钢琴前，自有人替她翻谱，点这支歌那支歌；她手帕掉了，自有人替她拾起来；出门闲逛、采花，也自有人做伴。她还学会了在静水潭里和急流下面甩假蝇，学会了别让丝钓线和肠衣脑线缠上灌木丛。
杰克·海明威懒得教新手，多半独自去钓鱼，要么索性不去，这就给了内德·巴什福德充裕的时间，把洛蕾塔当作一个表象来从容打量。作为表象，她完全合乎他的哲学的一切要求。她的蓝眼睛有男孩子那种直率的凝视，他从自己的深刻里领略这双眼睛，一面强忍着不打寒噤——他的哲学叫他相信，那眼波深处潜伏着口是心非。她有细瘦花朵的韵致，有细瓷那样颜色与线条的纤脆，这一切都叫他大大受用，全不去想底下搏动着的生命之力，也不管萧伯纳怎么说——萧伯纳他倒是信的。
洛蕾塔抽枝展叶了。她飞快地长出了个性。她发现自己有了自己的意志、自己的心愿，不再时时刻刻同黛西的意志心愿绞成一团。杰克·海明威宠她，艾丽斯·海明威惯她，内德·巴什福德殷勤地侍候她。他们纵容她的任性，笑看她的傻气，她也就养出一切漂亮娇柔的女人心里都潜伏着的那几手漂亮的小暴虐。她所处的环境像一剂安眠药，压住了她那点"永远同黛西住在一处"的古老心愿。这心愿不再像同比利做伴那阵子那样戳着她了。同比利见得越多，她越拿得准自己离不开黛西；同内德·巴什福德见得越多，她越忘记自己对黛西那种火烧火燎的需要。

===Original===
Ned Bashford likewise did some forgetting. He confused superficiality with profundity, and entangled appearance with reality until he accounted them one. Loretta was different from other women. There was no masquerade about her. She was real. He said as much to Mrs. Hemingway, and more, who agreed with him and at the same time caught her husband’s eyelid drooping down for the moment in an unmistakable wink.
It was at this time that Loretta received a letter from Billy that was somewhat different from his others. In the main, like all his letters, it was pathological. It was a long recital of symptoms and sufferings, his nervousness, his sleeplessness, and the state of his heart. Then followed reproaches, such as he had never made before. They were sharp enough to make her weep, and true enough to put tragedy into her face. This tragedy she carried down to the breakfast table. It made Jack and Mrs. Hemingway speculative, and it worried Ned. They glanced to him for explanation, but he shook his head.
“I’ll find out tonight,” Mrs. Hemingway said to her husband.

===Chinese===
内德·巴什福德也同样忘了些东西。他把肤浅同深刻搅混了，把表象同实在缠成一团，索性认作一回事。洛蕾塔跟别的女人不一样。她身上没有化装舞会。她是真的。他对海明威太太这么说了，还多说了一层；海明威太太深表赞同，同时恰好撞见她丈夫的眼皮垂下来一瞬，明白无误地眨了一下。
正是在这时候，洛蕾塔收到比利一封信，同往常的不大一样。大体上，它同比利的所有信一样，是一份病志。长长一篇陈述症状与苦楚：他的神经、他的失眠、他那颗心脏的近况。跟着是责备，这是他从前从来没有过的。那些话尖利得叫她落泪，又真切得叫她脸上带出悲剧来。她把这悲剧带下了早餐桌。它叫杰克和海明威太太满腹狐疑，叫内德悬心。他们朝他递眼色，要个解释，他摇了摇头。
"今晚我来问个明白，"海明威太太对丈夫说。

===Original===
But Ned caught Loretta in the afternoon in the big living-room. She tried to turn away. He caught her hands, and she faced him with wet lashes and trembling lips. He looked at her, silently and kindly. The lashes grew wetter.
“There, there, don’t cry, little one,” he said soothingly.
He put his arm protectingly around her shoulder. And to his shoulder, like a tired child, she turned her face. He thrilled in ways unusual for a Greek who has recovered from the long sickness.
“Oh, Ned,” she sobbed on his shoulder, “if you only knew how wicked I am!”

===Chinese===
可是当天下午，内德在大客厅里截住了洛蕾塔。她想扭头走开。他抓住她的双手，她只得面对他，睫毛上挂着泪，嘴唇发着颤。他望着她，无言，而和善。睫毛上的泪越挂越重。
"好了，好了，别哭，小家伙，"他柔声哄道。
他伸出一条胳膊，护住她的肩。她像个倦透了的孩子，把脸转向他的肩头。他心头一荡，荡出种种异样——这在一个从沉疴里康复过来的希腊人，是不多见的。
"唉，内德，"她伏在他肩上抽噎，"你要是知道我有多坏就好了！"

===Original===
He smiled indulgently, and breathed in a great breath freighted with the fragrance of her hair. He thought of his world-experience of women, and drew another long breath. There seemed to emanate from her the perfect sweetness of a child﻿—“the aura of a white soul,” was the way he phrased it to himself.
Then he noticed that her sobs were increasing.
“What’s the matter, little one?” he asked pettingly and almost paternally. “Has Jack been bullying you? Or has your dearly beloved sister failed to write?”
She did not answer, and he felt that he really must kiss her hair, that he could not be responsible if the situation continued much longer.

===Chinese===
他纵容地笑了笑，深深吸进一口气，满满载着她头发的香气。他想起自己对女人们的世故阅历，又深深吸了一口。她身上仿佛散发出一个孩子的十足的甜美——"一个白色灵魂的气韵"，他心里是这样措辞的。
接着他察觉，她的抽噎越来越凶了。
"怎么了，小家伙？"他疼爱地、几乎是慈父般地问。"杰克欺负你了？还是你那位亲爱的大姐忘了写信？"
她不答。他觉得实在该亲一亲她的头发了，这局面再僵下去，他可担待不起。

===Original===
“Tell me,” he said gently, “and we’ll see what I can do.”
“I can’t. You will despise me.﻿—Oh, Ned, I am so ashamed!”
He laughed incredulously, and lightly touched her hair with his lips﻿—so lightly that she did not know.
“Dear little one, let us forget all about it, whatever it is. I want to tell you how I love﻿—”

===Chinese===
"告诉我，"他温存地说，"看看我能帮上什么忙。"
"我说不出口。你会看不起我的。——唉，内德，我臊死了！"
他不信地笑了，嘴唇轻轻擦了一下她的头发——轻得她毫无知觉。
"亲爱的小家伙，不管是什么，咱们统统忘掉吧。我要告诉你我是多么爱——"

===Original===
She uttered a sharp cry that was all delight, and then moaned﻿—
“Too late!”
“Too late?” he echoed in surprise.
“Oh, why did I? Why did I?” she was moaning.

===Chinese===
她迸出一声短促的欢呼，满是欢喜，随即又呻吟起来——
"太迟了！"
"太迟了？"他诧异地应了一声。
"唉，我怎么会呢？怎么会呢？"她呻吟着。

===Original===
He was aware of a swift chill at his heart.
“What?” he asked.
“Oh, I﻿ ﻿… he﻿ ﻿… Billy.
“I am such a wicked woman, Ned. I know you will never speak to me again.”

===Chinese===
他觉得心口掠过一阵疾速的寒意。
"什么？"他问。
"唉，我……他……比利。
"我是个坏女人，内德，坏得可怕的女人。我知道你再也不会理我了。"

===Original===
“This﻿—er﻿—this Billy,” he began haltingly. “He is your brother?”
“No﻿ ﻿… he﻿ ﻿… I didn’t know. I was so young. I could not help it. Oh, I shall go mad! I shall go mad!”
It was then that Loretta felt his shoulder and the encircling arm become limp. He drew away from her gently, and gently he deposited her in a big chair, where she buried her face and sobbed afresh. He twisted his moustache fiercely, then drew up another chair and sat down.
“I﻿—I do not understand,” he said.

===Chinese===
"这位——呃——这位比利，"他结结巴巴地开口，"他是你哥哥？"
"不是……他……我那时不懂。我太小了。我管不住自己。唉，我要疯了！我要疯了！"
就在这时，洛蕾塔觉得他的肩头和环着她的那条胳膊瘫软了下去。他轻轻从她身边抽出身来，又轻轻把她安进一把大圈椅，她把脸埋进去，重新哭起来。他狠狠地捻着小胡子，然后拖过另一把椅子坐下。
"我——我不明白，"他说。

===Original===
“I am so unhappy,” she wailed.
“Why unhappy?”
“Because﻿ ﻿… he﻿ ﻿… he wants me to marry him.”
His face cleared on the instant, and he placed a hand soothingly on hers.

===Chinese===
"我好苦啊，"她哀声哭道。
"苦什么？"
"因为……他……他要我嫁给他。"
他的脸色霎时开朗了，一只手安慰地搭上她的手。

===Original===
“That should not make any girl unhappy,” he remarked sagely. “Because you don’t love him is no reason﻿—of course, you don’t love him?”
Loretta shook her head and shoulders in a vigorous negative.
“What?”
Bashford wanted to make sure.

===Chinese===
"这个可不该叫哪个姑娘犯愁，"他一副智者的口吻。"你不爱他，并不是理由——当然，你不爱他吧？"
洛蕾塔把头和肩膀一齐狠狠地摇，表示否认。
"什么？"
巴什福德要问个明白。

===Original===
“No,” she asserted explosively. “I don’t love Billy! I don’t want to love Billy!”
“Because you don’t love him,” Bashford resumed with confidence, “is no reason that you should be unhappy just because he has proposed to you.”
She sobbed again, and from the midst of her sobs she cried﻿—
“That’s the trouble. I wish I did love him. Oh, I wish I were dead!”

===Chinese===
"不爱，"她斩钉截铁地炸出一句。"我不爱比利！我也不想爱比利！"
"你不爱他，"巴什福德恢复了自信，接下去说，"并不是你该犯愁的理由，哪怕他向你求了婚。"
她又抽噎起来，抽噎当中哭喊道——
"毛病正在这儿。我倒巴望自己爱他。唉，我死了倒干净！"

===Original===
“Now, my dear child, you are worrying yourself over trifles.” His other hand crossed over after its mate and rested on hers. “Women do it every day. Because you have changed your mind or did not know your mind, because you have﻿—to use an unnecessarily harsh word﻿—jilted a man﻿—”
“Jilted!” She had raised her head and was looking at him with tear-dimmed eyes. “Oh, Ned, if that were all!”
“All?” he asked in a hollow voice, while his hands slowly retreated from hers. He was about to speak further, then remained silent.
“But I don’t want to marry him,” Loretta broke forth protestingly.

===Chinese===
"嗐，我亲爱的孩子，你这是拿鸡毛蒜皮折磨自己。"他另一只手也跟着搭过去，覆在她的手上。"姑娘们天天都这么干。你变卦了，或者原本就没拿定主意，你——用个未免重了点的字眼——甩了一个男人——"
"甩了！"她抬起头，泪眼朦胧地望着他。"唉，内德，要是仅止于此就好了！"
"仅止于此？"他声音发空地问，两只手从她的手上慢慢撤了回去。他还想再说什么，终究没说。
"可我并不想嫁给他呀，"洛蕾塔抗声分辩。

===Original===
“Then I shouldn’t,” he counselled.
“But I ought to marry him.”
“*Ought* to marry him?”
She nodded.

===Chinese===
"那就别嫁，"他出主意。
"可我应当嫁他。"
"*应当*嫁他？"
她点点头。

===Original===
“That is a strong word.”
“I know it is,” she acquiesced, while she strove to control her trembling lips. Then she spoke more calmly. “I am a wicked woman, a terribly wicked woman. No one knows how wicked I am﻿—except Billy.”
There was a pause. Ned Bashford’s face was grave, and he looked queerly at Loretta.
“He﻿—Billy knows?” he asked finally.

===Chinese===
"这可是个分量很重的字眼。"
"我知道重，"她认了，一面竭力止住嘴唇的颤抖。随后她说得平静了些。"我是个坏女人，坏得可怕的女人。没人知道我有多坏——除了比利。"
一时无语。内德·巴什福德神色凝重，古怪地打量着洛蕾塔。
"他——比利知道？"他终于问。

===Original===
A reluctant nod and flaming cheeks was the reply.
He debated with himself for a while, seeming, like a diver, to be preparing himself for the plunge.
“Tell me about it.” He spoke very firmly. “You must tell me all of it.”
“And will you﻿—ever﻿—forgive me?” she asked in a faint, small voice.

===Chinese===
回答他的，是勉强的一点头，和一双烧红的脸颊。
他心里掂量了一会儿，像个跳水的人，像是在做入水前的准备。
"讲给我听。"他说得非常坚决。"你必须原原本本讲给我听。"
"那你会——永远——饶了我吗？"她用细细的、小小的声音问。

===Original===
He hesitated, drew a long breath, and made the plunge.
“Yes,” he said desperately. “I’ll forgive you. Go ahead.”
“There was no one to tell me,” she began. “We were with each other so much. I did not know anything of the world﻿—then.”
She paused to meditate. Bashford was biting his lip impatiently.

===Chinese===
他犹豫了一下，深深吸了口气，一头扎了下去。
"会，"他豁出去了。"我饶你。说吧。"
"没有人告诉我呀，"她开了头。"我们老在一块儿。那时候，世事我一样也不懂。"
她停下来寻思。巴什福德不耐烦地咬着嘴唇。

===Original===
“If I had only known﻿—”
She paused again.
“Yes, go on,” he urged.
“We were together almost every evening.”

===Chinese===
"我要是早知道——"
她又停住了。
"嗯，说下去，"他催促。
"差不多每天晚上我们都在一块儿。"

===Original===
“Billy?” he demanded, with a savageness that startled her.
“Yes, of course, Billy. We were with each other so much﻿ ﻿… If I had only known﻿ ﻿… There was no one to tell me﻿ ﻿… I was so young﻿—”
Her lips parted as though to speak further, and she regarded him anxiously.
“The scoundrel!”

===Chinese===
"同比利？"他追问，口气凶得吓了她一跳。
"是啊，当然是比利。我们老在一块儿……我要是早知道……没有人告诉我……我那时太小了——"
她双唇微启，像还有话，不安地望着他。
"这个恶棍！"

===Original===
With the explosion Ned Bashford was on his feet, no longer a tired Greek, but a violently angry young man.
“Billy is not a scoundrel; he is a good man,” Loretta defended, with a firmness that surprised Bashford.
“I suppose you’ll be telling me next that it was all your fault,” he said sarcastically.
She nodded.

===Chinese===
这一炸，内德·巴什福德一跃而起，不再是疲惫的希腊人，而是一个怒气冲冲的年轻人。
"比利不是恶棍，他是个好人，"洛蕾塔辩护，那份坚定叫巴什福德吃了一惊。
"我猜你接下来就要告诉我，全是你自己的错了，"他讥讽道。
她点点头。

===Original===
“What?” he shouted.
“It was all my fault,” she said steadily. “I should never have let him. I was to blame.”
Bashford ceased from his pacing up and down, and when he spoke, his voice was resigned.
“All right,” he said. “I don’t blame you in the least, Loretta. And you have been very honest. But Billy is right, and you are wrong. You must get married.”

===Chinese===
"什么？"他吼起来。
"全是我的错，"她平静地说。"我根本就不该由着他。怪我。"
巴什福德停止了来回踱步，开口时，声音认了命。
"好吧，"他说。"我一点不怪你，洛蕾塔。你也算得很诚实了。可是比利对，你错。你们必须结婚。"

===Original===
“To Billy?” she asked, in a dim, faraway voice.
“Yes, to Billy. I’ll see to it. Where does he live? I’ll make him.”
“But I don’t want to marry Billy!” she cried out in alarm. “Oh, Ned, you won’t do that?”
“I shall,” he answered sternly. “You must. And Billy must. Do you understand?”

===Chinese===
"同比利？"她问，声音幽幽的，像从很远的地方来。
"对，同比利。这事我来办。他住哪儿？我会叫他娶的。"
"可我不想同比利结婚呀！"她惊惶地叫起来。"唉，内德，你不会那么做吧？"
"我会的，"他严厉地答。"你必须。比利也必须。你明白吗？"

===Original===
Loretta buried her face in the cushioned chair back, and broke into a passionate storm of sobs.
All that Bashford could make out at first, as he listened, was: “But I don’t want to leave Daisy! I don’t want to leave Daisy!”
He paced grimly back and forth, then stopped curiously to listen.
“How was I to know?﻿—Boo﻿—hoo,” Loretta was crying. “He didn’t tell me. Nobody else ever kissed me. I never dreamed a kiss could be so terrible﻿ ﻿… until, boo-hoo﻿ ﻿… until he wrote to me. I only got the letter this morning.”

===Chinese===
洛蕾塔把脸埋进圈椅的软靠背，爆发出一阵痛哭。
他听着，起初只听清一句："可我不想离开黛西！我不想离开黛西！"
他铁青着脸来回踱步，随后停住脚，好奇地听。
"我哪知道呢？——呜——呜，"洛蕾塔哭着。"他没有告诉我呀。别人谁也没亲过我。我做梦也没想到一个吻会这么可怕……直到，呜呜……直到他写了信给我。那封信我今天早上才收到。"

===Original===
His face brightened. It seemed as though light was dawning on him.
“Is that what you’re crying about?”
“N﻿—no.”
His heart sank.

===Chinese===
他脸上放光了。他像是有点开窍了。
"你哭的就是这个？"
"不——不是。"
他的心沉了下去。

===Original===
“Then what are you crying about?” he asked in a hopeless voice.
“Because you said I had to marry Billy. And I don’t want to marry Billy. I don’t want to leave Daisy. I don’t know what I want. I wish I were dead.”
He nerved himself for another effort.
“Now look here, Loretta, be sensible. What is this about kisses. You haven’t told me everything?”

===Chinese===
"那你哭的是什么？"他声音里没了指望。
"因为你说我必须同比利结婚。可我不想同比利结婚。我不想离开黛西。我也不知道自己想要什么。我死了倒好。"
他鼓起劲，再试一回。
"你听我说，洛蕾塔，讲点道理。这亲嘴到底是怎么回事。你没有全告诉我吧？"

===Original===
“I﻿—I don’t want to tell you everything.”
She looked at him beseechingly in the silence that fell.
“Must I?” she quavered finally.
“You must,” he said imperatively. “You must tell me everything.”

===Chinese===
"我——我不想什么都告诉你。"
一阵沉默里，她哀告地望着他。
"非说不可吗？"她终于颤声问。
"非说不可，"他斩钉截铁。"你必须原原本本告诉我。"

===Original===
“Well, then﻿ ﻿… must I?”
“You must.”
“He﻿ ﻿… I﻿ ﻿… we﻿ ﻿…” she began flounderingly. Then blurted out, “I let him, and he kissed me.”
“Go on,” Bashford commanded desperately.

===Chinese===
"那么……非说不可？"
"非说不可。"
"他……我……我们……"她狼狈地开了头，随后脱口而出："我由着他，他就亲了我。"
"说下去，"巴什福德豁出命地命令。

===Original===
“That’s all,” she answered.
“All?” There was a vast incredulity in his voice.
“All?” In her voice was an interrogation no less vast.
“I mean﻿—er﻿—nothing worse?” He was overwhelmingly aware of his own awkwardness.

===Chinese===
"就这些，"她答。
"就这些？"他的话音里满是不信。
"就这些？"她的声音里，问号也不小。
"我是说——呃——没有更出格的？"他狼狈透了，只觉得自己笨口拙舌。

===Original===
“Worse?” She was frankly puzzled. “As though there could be! Billy said﻿—”
“When did he say it?” Bashford demanded abruptly.
“In his letter I got this morning. Billy said that my﻿ ﻿… our﻿ ﻿… our kisses were terrible if we didn’t get married.”
Bashford’s head was swimming.

===Chinese===
"更出格？"她当真糊涂了。"哪还能有更出格的！比利说——"
"他什么时候说的？"巴什福德猛地问。
"就在我今天早上收到的那封信里。比利说，我们要是不结婚，我的……我们的……我们的亲嘴就是造孽。"
巴什福德的脑袋晕了。

===Original===
“What else did Billy say?” he asked.
“He said that when a woman allowed a man to kiss her, she always married him﻿—that it was terrible if she didn’t. It was the custom, he said; and I say it is a bad, wicked custom, and I don’t like it. I know I’m terrible,” she added defiantly, “but I can’t help it.”
Bashford absentmindedly brought out a cigarette.
“Do you mind if I smoke?” he asked, as he struck a match.

===Chinese===
"比利还说了什么？"他问。
"他说，一个女人要是由得男人亲她，她就得嫁他——不嫁就造孽。他说这是规矩；我说这是又坏又恶的规矩，我不喜欢。我知道我造孽，"她倔强地添了一句，"可我管不住。"
巴什福德心不在焉地摸出一支烟。
"我抽支烟，你不介意吧？"他一面划火柴一面问。

===Original===
Then he came to himself.
“I beg your pardon,” he cried, flinging away match and cigarette. “I don’t want to smoke. I didn’t mean that at all. What I mean is﻿—”
He bent over Loretta, caught her hands in his, then sat on the arm of the chair and softly put one arm around her.
“Loretta, I am a fool. I mean it. And I mean something more. I want you to be my wife.”

===Chinese===
随即他回过神来。
"恕我失礼，"他叫道，把火柴和烟一齐扔了。"我不想抽烟。我完全不是那个意思。我是说——"
他俯身向着洛蕾塔，双手握住她的手，然后坐上椅子扶手，轻轻用一条胳膊揽住她。
"洛蕾塔，我是个傻子。我说的是真心话。我还有更要紧的话说。我要你做我的妻子。"

===Original===
He waited anxiously in the pause that followed.
“You might answer me,” he urged.
“I will﻿ ﻿… if﻿—”
“Yes, go on. If what?”

===Chinese===
接下来一阵静默，他焦灼地等着。
"你倒是回我一句呀，"他催道。
"我愿意……要是——"
"嗯，说下去。要是怎样？"

===Original===
“If I don’t have to marry Billy.”
“You can’t marry both of us,” he almost shouted.
“And it isn’t the custom﻿ ﻿… what﻿ ﻿… what Billy said?”
“No, it isn’t the custom. Now, Loretta, will you marry me?”

===Chinese===
"要是我不必同比利结婚。"
"总不能我们俩你都嫁，"他几乎喊起来。
"那也不是规矩……比利说的……他说的那个？"
"对，那不是规矩。好了，洛蕾塔，你肯嫁我吗？"

===Original===
“Don’t be angry with me,” she pouted demurely.
He gathered her into his arms and kissed her.
“I wish it were the custom,” she said in a faint voice, from the midst of the embrace, “because then I’d have to marry you, Ned dear﻿ ﻿… wouldn’t I?”

===Chinese===
"别生我的气嘛，"她娇憨地撅起嘴。
他把她搂进怀里，吻了她。
"我倒巴不得那是规矩呢，"她偎在拥抱当中，声音细细地说，"因为那样我就非嫁你不可了，亲爱的内德……是不是？"

## “Just Meat” / 纯为财货

===Original===
He strolled to the corner and glanced up and down the intersecting street, but saw nothing save the oases of light shed by the street lamps at the successive crossings. Then he strolled back the way he had come. He was a shadow of a man, sliding noiselessly and without undue movement through the semidarkness. Also he was very alert, like a wild animal in the jungle, keenly perceptive and receptive. The movement of another in the darkness about him would need to have been more shadowy than he to have escaped him.
In addition to the running advertisement of the state of affairs carried to him by his senses, he had a subtler perception, a *feel*, of the atmosphere around him. He knew that the house in front of which he paused for a moment, contained children. Yet by no willed effort of perception did he have this knowledge. For that matter, he was not even aware that he knew, so occult was the impression. Yet, did a moment arise in which action, in relation to that house, were imperative, he would have acted on the assumption that it contained children. He was not aware of all that he knew about the neighbourhood.
In the same way, he knew not how, he knew that no danger threatened in the footfalls that came up the cross street. Before he saw the walker, he knew him for a belated pedestrian hurrying home. The walker came into view at the crossing and disappeared on up the street. The man that watched, noted a light that flared up in the window of a house on the corner, and as it died down he knew it for an expiring match. This was conscious identification of familiar phenomena, and through his mind flitted the thought, “Wanted to know what time.” In another house one room was lighted. The light burned dimly and steadily, and he had the feel that it was a sickroom.

===Chinese===
他踱到街角，朝交叉的街上两头张了张，什么也没看见，只有一盏盏路口的街灯洒下的一小片一小片光的绿洲。他又顺着来路踱了回去。他是一道人影，在半明半暗里无声地滑行，没有一丝多余的动作。他又极警觉，像丛林里的野兽，感觉得又尖、收得又快。黑暗里另有谁要挪动，除非比他更像影子，否则休想逃过他。
感官源源不断地把周围的情形报给他，除此之外，他还有一种更细微的知觉，一种对四周气氛的*感觉*。他知道他在门前稍停的那户人家里有孩子。可这知识并不是他着意察觉得来的。说起来，他甚至不觉得自己知道——这印象隐秘到了这地步。然而，倘若有哪个当口真到了非对那房子下手不可的地步，他行动时自会拿准那家里有孩子。这左近一带他知道的事，他自己也未必清楚。
同样地，他不知怎么就知道了，横街上过来的脚步声不带凶险。人还没露面，他已断定那是个赶迟了的路人，正急着回家。那人在路口现身，又顺着街远了。守望的人注意到街角一户人家的窗里火光一闪，火光敛下去时，他知道那是一根划着又灭了的火柴。这是对熟识现象的清醒辨认，一个念头打他心头掠过：“想知道几点钟了。”另一户人家有一间屋亮着灯。灯光幽暗而平稳，他觉出那是一间病房。

===Original===
He was especially interested in a house across the street in the middle of the block. To this house he paid most attention. No matter what way he looked, nor what way he walked, his looks and his steps always returned to it. Except for an open window above the porch, there was nothing unusual about the house. Nothing came in nor out. Nothing happened. There were no lighted windows, nor had lights appeared and disappeared in any of the windows. Yet it was the central point of his consideration. He rallied to it each time after a divination of the state of the neighbourhood.
Despite his feel of things, he was not confident. He was supremely conscious of the precariousness of his situation. Though unperturbed by the footfalls of the chance pedestrian, he was as keyed up and sensitive and ready to be startled as any timorous deer. He was aware of the possibility of other intelligences prowling about in the darkness﻿—intelligences similar to his own in movement, perception, and divination.
Far down the street he caught a glimpse of something that moved. And he knew it was no late home-goer, but menace and danger. He whistled twice to the house across the street, then faded away shadow-like to the corner and around the corner. Here he paused and looked about him carefully. Reassured, he peered back around the corner and studied the object that moved and that was coming nearer. He had divined aright. It was a policeman.

===Chinese===
他对街对面、这一段街正中的那座房子格外上心。他最多的心思都花在它身上。不管朝哪边看，不管往哪头走，他的眼光和脚步总归回到它那儿。除了门廊上方一扇敞着的窗，这房子毫无异样。没有东西进去，也没有东西出来。什么都没有发生。没有点灯的窗，也没有哪扇窗里灯光亮起又熄灭过。可它就是他盘算的中心。每一次掐算完左近的情形，他都会收回到它身上来。
别看他有这份对事情的直觉，他心里并没有底。他极其清醒地意识到自己处境的凶险。偶然路过的脚步声惊不动他，可他绷得紧紧的、敏感到极点、随时会受惊，同哪一头胆小的鹿都不相上下。他知道黑暗里可能有别的灵物在游荡——在行动、知觉和占算上同他一路的灵物。
长街远处，他瞥见有什么东西在动。他知道那不是晚归的行人，而是威胁和危险。他朝街对面的房子吹了两声口哨，随即影子似的隐到街角，拐了过去。他在这里停住，仔细朝四下里望。放心之后，他从街角探回头去，端详那个动着、并且越走越近的东西。他掐算得不错。是个警察。

===Original===
The man went down the cross street to the next corner, from the shelter of which he watched the corner he had just left. He saw the policeman pass by, going straight on up the street. He paralleled the policeman’s course, and from the next corner again watched him go by; then he returned the way he had come. He whistled once to the house across the street, and after a time whistled once again. There was reassurance in the whistle, just as there had been warning in the previous double whistle.
He saw a dark bulk outline itself on the roof of the porch and slowly descend a pillar. Then it came down the steps, passed through the small iron gate, and went down the sidewalk, taking on the form of a man. He that watched kept on his own side of the street and moved on abreast to the corner, where he crossed over and joined the other. He was quite small alongside the man he accosted.
“How’d you make out, Matt?” he asked.

===Chinese===
这人顺着横街走到下一个街角，借街角的遮蔽盯住他刚离开的那个街角。他看见警察走了过去，径直顺着长街去了。他与警察的路线平行着走，又从下一个街角望他走远；然后他顺原路折了回去。他朝街对面的房子吹了一声口哨，隔了一阵，又吹了一声。这口哨里报的是平安，正如先前那两声口哨里报的是警讯。
他看见一团黑乎乎的块影在门廊顶上现出轮廓，顺一根廊柱缓缓降下来。接着它下了台阶，穿过小铁门，上了人行道，一路走一路化出个人形。守望的人待在自己这侧的街上，与它并排走到街角，在那儿过了街，同对方会合。同他招呼的这个人比起来，他显得相当瘦小。
“事情办得怎么样，马特？”他问。

===Original===
The other grunted indistinctly, and walked on in silence a few steps.
“I reckon I landed the goods,” he said.
Jim chuckled in the darkness, and waited for further information. The blocks passed by under their feet, and he grew impatient.
“Well, how about them goods?” he asked. “What kind of a haul did you make, anyway?”

===Chinese===
对方含混地哼了一声，闷头走了几步。
“我寻思货是到手了，”他说。
吉姆在黑暗里低笑，等着下文。一个街区一个街区从脚底下过去，他等得心焦了。
“喂，货到底怎么样？”他问。“你这趟究竟捞了多少？”

===Original===
“I was too busy to figger it out, but it’s fat. I can tell you that much, Jim, it’s fat. I don’t dast to think how fat it is. Wait till we get to the room.”
Jim looked at him keenly under the street lamp of the next crossing, and saw that his face was a trifle grim and that he carried his left arm peculiarly.
“What’s the matter with your arm?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
“我忙得顾不上细算，可这票肥。我能告诉你这么多，吉姆，肥得很。肥到什么地步我都不敢想。等回了屋子再说。”
在下一个路口的街灯底下，吉姆锐利地打量他，看出他脸色带着几分狠相，左臂的架势也有些不对。
“你胳膊怎么了？”他问。

===Original===
“The little cuss bit me. Hope I don’t get hydrophoby. Folks gets hydrophoby from manbite sometimes, don’t they?”
“Gave you fight, eh?” Jim asked encouragingly.
The other grunted.
“You’re harder’n hell to get information from,” Jim burst out irritably. “Tell us about it. You ain’t goin’ to lose money just a-tellin’ a guy.”

===Chinese===
“那个小兔崽子咬了我一口。但愿别得上狂犬病。人咬的伤有时候也教人得狂犬病，是不是？”
“他跟你动手了，嗯？”吉姆搭腔，一副巴望热闹的口气。
对方哼了一声。
“跟你打听点事，比下地狱还难，”吉姆憋不住火了。“讲讲呗。跟哥们说说又不少挣一个子儿。”

===Original===
“I guess I choked him some,” came the answer. Then, by way of explanation, “He woke up on me.”
“You did it neat. I never heard a sound.”
“Jim,” the other said with seriousness, “it’s a hangin’ matter. I fixed ’m. I had to. He woke up on me. You an’ me’s got to do some layin’ low for a spell.”

===Chinese===
“我大概掐了他几下，”回答来了这么一句。随后又补了句解释：“他撞上我，醒了。”
“你办得干净利落。我一点动静都没听见。”
“吉姆，”对方换了正经口气，“这是掉脑袋的案子。我把他结果了。不得不结果。他撞上我，醒了。你我俩得避一阵子风头。”

===Original===
Jim gave a low whistle of comprehension.
“Did you hear me whistle?” he asked suddenly.
“Sure. I was all done. I was just comin’ out.”
“It was a bull. But he wasn’t on a little bit. Went right by an’ kept a-paddin’ the hoof out a sight. Then I come back an’ gave you the whistle. What made you take so long after that?”

===Chinese===
吉姆低低吹了声口哨，表示明白了。
“你听见我吹口哨没有？”他突然问。
“听见了。我全办完了，正要出来。”
“来了个条子。可他一点没起疑。径直走了过去，一路巡出去老远。我才折回来，给你吹了哨。打那以后你怎么耽搁那么久？”

===Original===
“I was waitin’ to make sure,” Matt explained. “I was mighty glad when I heard you whistle again. It’s hard work waitin’. I just sat there an’ thought an’ thought﻿ ﻿… oh, all kinds of things. It’s remarkable what a fellow’ll think about. And then there was a darn cat that kept movin’ around the house all’ botherin’ me with its noises.”
“An’ it’s fat!” Jim exclaimed irrelevantly and with joy.
“I’m sure tellin’ you, Jim, it’s fat. I’m plum’ anxious for another look at ’em.”

===Chinese===
“我等着拿个准，”马特解释道。“再听见你吹哨，我高兴坏了。等着是个苦差事。我就那么坐着，想啊想……哦，什么乱七八糟的都想。人在这时候会想些什么，真是了不得。还有一只该死的猫，围着房子转来转去，拿动静搅我。”
“而且这票肥！”吉姆欢喜地岔开了一句。
“我实打实告诉你，吉姆，肥。我急着再瞅它们一眼呢。”

===Original===
Unconsciously the two men quickened their pace. Yet they did not relax from their caution. Twice they changed their course in order to avoid policemen, and they made very sure that they were not observed when they dived into the dark hallway of a cheap rooming house down town.
Not until they had gained their own room on the top floor, did they scratch a match. While Jim lighted a lamp, Matt locked the door and threw the bolts into place. As he turned, he noticed that his partner was waiting expectantly. Matt smiled to himself at the other’s eagerness.
“Them searchlights is all right,” he said, drawing forth a small pocket electric lamp and examining it. “But we got to get a new battery. It’s runnin’ pretty weak. I thought once or twice it’d leave me in the dark. Funny arrangements in that house. I near got lost. His room was on the left, an’ that fooled me some.”

===Chinese===
两个人不知不觉加快了脚步。可警惕一分没松。他们两回改道躲警察，等到一头扎进市中心一家廉价公寓的黑门厅时，还十拿九稳地确认了没人看见。
直到进了顶楼自己那间屋，他们才划着火柴。吉姆点灯，马特锁门、插好门闩。他转过身，察觉同伙正眼巴巴等着。马特瞧着对方这副猴急，暗自一笑。
“这探照灯还成，”他说着，掏出一支小小的电筒端详。“不过得换新电池了。光弱得很。有一两回我都当它要把我撂在黑地里。那房子里的格局邪门。我差点迷了路。他房间在左边，这就把我坑得不轻。”

===Original===
“I told you it was on the left,” Jim interrupted.
“You told me it was on the right,” Matt went on. “I guess I know what you told me, an’ there’s the map you drew.”
Fumbling in his vest pocket, he drew out a folded slip of paper. As he unfolded it, Jim bent over and looked.

===Chinese===
“我明明告诉你是在左边，”吉姆插嘴。
“你告诉我的是在右边，”马特接着说。“你说了什么我总还记得，再说你画的那张图不就在那儿。”
他在马甲口袋里摸索，抽出一张折起来的纸条。他展开的时候，吉姆凑过去看。

===Original===
“I did make a mistake,” he confessed.
“You sure did. It got me guessin’ some for a while.”
“But it don’t matter now,” Jim cried. “Let’s see what you got.”

===Chinese===
“我确实弄错了，”他认了。
“可不是嘛。害我猜疑了好一阵。”
“可如今不打紧了，”吉姆叫道。“看看你到手了什么。”

===Original===
“It does matter,” Matt retorted. “It matters a lot﻿ ﻿… to me. I’ve got to run all the risk. I put my head in the trap while you stay on the street. You got to get on to yourself an’ be more careful. All right, I’ll show you.”
He dipped loosely into his trousers pocket and brought out a handful of small diamonds. He spilled them out in a blazing stream on the greasy table. Jim let out a great oath.
“That’s nothing,” Matt said with triumphant complacence. “I ain’t begun yet.”

===Chinese===
“打紧，”马特回敬。“打紧得很……对我而言。风险全得我一个人担。我把脑袋伸进夹子，你倒好，待在街上。你得给我长长记性，仔细点。行了，拿给你看。”
他松松地探进裤袋，抓出一把小钻石。他把它们一股脑倒在油腻的桌面上，淌成一道烈焰般的细流。吉姆狠狠地咒骂了一声。
“这算什么，”马特说，一副得胜又自得的神气。“还没开头呢。”

===Original===
From one pocket after another he continued bringing forth the spoil. There were many diamonds wrapped in chamois skin that were larger than those in the first handful. From one pocket he brought out a handful of very small cut gems.
“Sun dust,” he remarked, as he spilled them on the table in a space by themselves.
Jim examined them.
“Just the same, they retail for a couple of dollars each,” he said. “Is that all?”
“Ain’t it enough?” the other demanded in an aggrieved tone.
“Sure it is,” Jim answered with unqualified approval. “Better’n I expected. I wouldn’t take a cent less than ten thousan’ for the bunch.”

===Chinese===
他一个口袋接一个口袋，继续往外掏赃物。有许多麂皮包着的钻石，比头一把的都大。从一个口袋里，他掏出一把切割过的极小的宝石。
“太阳尘，”他说着，把它们单独倒在桌上一块空处。
吉姆端详它们。
“别看这样，零售也得两块钱一粒，”他说。“就这些？”
“这还不够？”对方委屈起来。
“够了，怎么不够，”吉姆答道，一万个赞成。“比我预想的强。这一堆少一个子儿一万块我都不卖。”

===Original===
“Ten thousan’,” Matt sneered. “They’re worth twic’t that, an’ I don’t know anything about joolery, either. Look at that big boy!”
He picked it out from the sparkling heap and held it near to the lamp with the air of an expert, weighing and judging.
“Worth a thousan’ all by its lonely,” was Jim’s quicker judgment.
“A thousan’ your grandmother,” was Matt’s scornful rejoinder. “You couldn’t buy it for three.”
“Wake me up! I’m dreamin’!” The sparkle of the gems was in Jim’s eyes, and he began sorting out the larger diamonds and examining them. “We’re rich men, Matt﻿—we’ll be regular swells.”
“It’ll take years to get rid of ’em,” was Matt’s more practical thought.

===Chinese===
“一万，”马特嗤笑。“值那个数的两倍，我还根本不懂珠宝呢。瞧那大块头！”
他从那堆闪闪发光里把它拈出来，凑到灯前，拿出一副行家的派头，掂量着、品评着。
“单它一个就值一千，”吉姆的判词下得更快。
“一千你个头，”马特轻蔑地回敬。“三千你也买不来。”
“快叫醒我！我在做梦吧！”吉姆眼里闪着宝石的光，动手挑出大颗的钻石，一颗颗看。“咱们是阔佬了，马特——要风风光光做人上人了。”
“脱手得脱上好几年，”马特想得更实。

===Original===
“But think how we’ll live! Nothin’ to do but spend the money an’ go on gettin’ rid of em.”
Matt’s eyes were beginning to sparkle, though sombrely, as his phlegmatic nature woke up.
“I told you I didn’t dast think how fat it was,” he murmured in a low voice.

===Chinese===
“可想想咱们要过的日子！啥也不用干，光管花钱，再把货一批批出掉。”
马特的眼睛也开始发亮了，虽然亮得阴沉——他那黏滞的性子醒过来了。
“我早说过，肥到什么地步我不敢想，”他低声咕哝。

===Original===
“What a killin’! What a killin’!” was the other’s more ecstatic utterance.
“I almost forgot,” Matt said, thrusting his hand into his inside coat pocket.
A string of large pearls emerged from wrappings of tissue paper and chamois skin. Jim scarcely glanced at them.

===Chinese===
“多横财！多横财！”另一个喊得更狂热。
“差点忘了，”马特说着，手插进上衣内袋。
一串大珍珠从薄纸和麂皮的包裹里露了出来。吉姆几乎没瞟它们一眼。

===Original===
“They’re worth money,” he said, and returned to the diamonds.
A silence fell on the two men. Jim played with the gems, running them through his fingers, sorting them into piles, and spreading them out flat and wide. He was a slender, weazened man, nervous, irritable, high-strung, and anaemic﻿—a typical child of the gutter, with unbeautiful twisted features, small-eyed, with face and mouth perpetually and feverishly hungry, brutish in a catlike way, stamped to the core with degeneracy.
Matt did not finger the diamonds. He sat with chin on hands and elbows on table, blinking heavily at the blazing array. He was in every way a contrast to the other. No city had bred him. He was heavy-muscled and hairy, gorilla-like in strength and aspect. For him there was no unseen world. His eyes were full and wide apart, and there seemed in them a certain bold brotherliness. They inspired confidence. But a closer inspection would have shown that his eyes were just a trifle too full, just a shade too wide apart. He exceeded, spilled over the limits of normality, and his features told lies about the man beneath.

===Chinese===
“这值钱，”他说，又回去弄钻石了。
两个人都沉默了。吉姆摆弄着宝石，让它们从指缝里簌簌漏过，分成一堆堆，又摊开摊平摊宽。他是个干瘦萎缩的男人，神经质，易怒，一惊一乍，贫血——阴沟里长出来的典型产物，五官歪扭而不美，眼睛小，脸和嘴永远发着烧似的饿着，带一种猫样的兽性，从里到外都打着堕落的烙印。
马特不去碰钻石。他坐着，下巴搁在手上，胳膊肘支在桌上，沉甸甸地眨着眼看那一片火光。他处处同另一个相反。没有哪座城市生养过他。他肌肉厚重，多毛，力气和模样都像猩猩。在他那里没有看不见的世界。他一双眼睛圆鼓鼓的，分得很开，眼里似乎有一种莽直的手足情，教人信赖。可细看便会发现，那双眼睛实在鼓得过了头，分得也实在开得过了头。他越了界，从正常人的限度里漫了出来，那张脸替底下的那个人撒着谎。

===Original===
“The bunch is worth fifty thousan’,” Jim remarked suddenly.
“A hundred thousan’,” Matt said.
The silence returned and endured a long time, to be broken again by Jim.

===Chinese===
“这一堆值五万，”吉姆突然开口。
“十万，”马特说。
沉默又回来了，僵了很久，才又被吉姆打破。

===Original===
“What in hell was he doin’ with ’em all at the house?﻿—that’s what I want to know. I’d a-thought he’d kept ’em in the safe down at the store.”
Matt had just been considering the vision of the throttled man as he had last looked upon him in the dim light of the electric lantern; but he did not start at the mention of him.
“There’s no tellin’,” he answered. “He might a-ben gettin’ ready to chuck his pardner. He might a-pulled out in the mornin’ for parts unknown, if we hadn’t happened along. I guess there’s just as many thieves among honest men as there is among thieves. You read about such things in the papers, Jim. Pardners is always knifin’ each other.”
A queer, nervous look came into the other’s eyes. Matt did not betray that he noted it, though he said﻿—

===Chinese===
“他到底把这一堆全搁在家里干什么？——我就纳闷这个。我还当他会收在铺子底下的保险柜里呢。”
马特方才正在回想那个被掐死的人——电筒昏光里他最后望见的那副模样；可提起这人，他脸都没变一下。
“说不准，”他答道。“他兴许正打算黑掉他的合伙人。要不是恰好叫咱们赶上，他兴许一早就拔脚去了不知什么地方。我算看透了，正经人里的贼，一点也不比贼窝里的少。这种事报上你见得多了，吉姆。合伙的，从来都是背后捅刀。”
另一个的眼里掠过一丝古怪的、发慌的神色。马特看见了却不动声色，只是说——

===Original===
“What was you thinkin’ about, Jim?”
Jim was a trifle awkward for the moment.
“Nothin’,” he answered. “Only I was thinkin’ just how funny it was﻿—all them jools at his house. What made you ask?”

===Chinese===
“你刚才在想什么，吉姆？”
吉姆一时有点讪讪的。
“没什么，”他答。“就是觉得这事怪得很——那么多珠宝居然在他家里。你问这个干什么？”

===Original===
“Nothin’. I was just wonderin’, that was all.”
The silence settled down, broken by an occasional low and nervous giggle on the part of Jim. He was overcome by the spread of gems. It was not that he felt their beauty. He was unaware that they were beautiful in themselves. But in them his swift imagination visioned the joys of life they would buy, and all the desires and appetites of his diseased mind and sickly flesh were tickled by the promise they extended. He builded wondrous, orgy-haunted castles out of their brilliant fires, and was appalled at what he builded. Then it was that he giggled. It was all too impossible to be real. And yet there they blazed on the table before him, fanning the flame of the lust of him, and he giggled again.
“I guess we might as well count ’em,” Matt said suddenly, tearing himself away from his own visions. “You watch me an’ see that it’s square, because you an’ me has got to be on the square, Jim. Understand?”

===Chinese===
“不干什么。就是随口一问，没别的。”
沉默落定，只偶尔被吉姆低低的一声神经质的窃笑打断。他被这一摊宝石压服了。倒不是他感到了它们的美。他根本不知道它们本身是美的。可是在它们身上，他那飞快的想象力望见了它们能买来的人间欢乐，他那病态头脑、病弱皮肉的一切欲望和胃口，都被它们许下的愿撩拨得痒痒的。他拿它们烈火般的光芒，搭起一座座住满荒淫的奇妙的城堡，又给自己搭出的东西吓一跳。于是他窃笑。这一切太不可能，不像真的。可它们分明就在眼前桌上烧着，把他体内的欲火越扇越旺，他便又窃笑起来。
“我看咱们还是点一点数，”马特突然说，把自己从幻景里扯了回来。“你盯着我点，看是不是公道，因为你我之间得公道，吉姆。懂吗？”

===Original===
Jim did not like this, and betrayed it in his eyes, while Matt did not like what he saw in his partner’s eyes.
“Understand?” Matt repeated, almost menacingly.
“Ain’t we always ben square?” the other replied, on the defensive because of the treachery already whispering in him.
“It don’t cost nothin’, bein’ square in hard times,” Matt retorted. “It’s bein’ square in prosperity that counts. When we ain’t got nothin’, we can’t help bein’ square. We’re prosperous now, an’ we’ve got to be business men﻿—honest business men. Understand?”
“That’s the talk for me,” Jim approved, but deep down in the meagre soul of him﻿—and in spite of him﻿—wanton and lawless thoughts were stirring like chained beasts.
Matt stepped to the food shelf behind the two-burner kerosene cooking stove. He emptied the tea from a paper bag, and from a second bag emptied some red peppers. Returning to the table with the bags, he put into them the two sizes of small diamonds. Then he counted the large gems and wrapped them in their tissue paper and chamois skin.

===Chinese===
吉姆不爱听这个，眼里露了出来，而马特瞧着同伙眼里那东西，心里也不痛快。
“懂吗？”马特又问了一遍，几乎是威胁了。
“咱们难道不是一向公道？”对方答道，摆出守势——背叛早已在他心里嘀咕上了。
“穷的时候讲公道，不值一个钱，”马特回敬。“难得的是发了财还讲公道。咱们一无所有的时候，想不公道也没本钱。如今咱们发起来了，就得做买卖人——老老实实的买卖人。懂吗？”
“这话合我的意，”吉姆赞成道，可是在他那点干瘪的灵魂深处——由不得他——种种放纵无法的念头，正像拴着的野兽一样蠢动。
马特走到双眼煤油炉后面的食品架跟前。他倒空一只纸袋里的茶叶，又倒空另一袋里的红辣椒。他拿着两只袋子回到桌边，把两种规格的小钻石分装进去。然后他点了大宝石的数，用薄纸和麂皮包好。

===Original===
“Hundred an’ forty-seven good-sized ones,” was his inventory; “twenty real big ones; two big boys and one whopper; an’ a couple of fistfuls of teeny ones an’ dust.”
He looked at Jim.
“Correct,” was the response.

===Chinese===
“一百四十七颗有尺寸的，”他报着账；“二十颗真正的大货；两颗大块头，外加一颗巨无霸；还有几把碎小的和粉子。”
他看了看吉姆。
“没错，”对方应道。

===Original===
He wrote the count out on a slip of memorandum paper, and made a copy of it, giving one slip to his partner and retaining the other.
“Just for reference,” he said.
Again he had recourse to the food shelf, where he emptied the sugar from a large paper bag. Into this he thrust the diamonds, large and small, wrapped it up in a bandana handkerchief, and stowed it away under his pillow. Then he sat down on the edge of the bed and took off his shoes.

===Chinese===
他把数目写在一张便条上，又誊了一份，一张给同伙，一张自己留着。
“留个凭据，”他说。
他又求助于食品架，倒空了一只大纸袋里的糖。钻石大小全塞进这只袋子，外头裹上一条印花大手帕，收进自己枕头底下。然后他在床沿坐下，脱鞋。

===Original===
“An’ you think they’re worth a hundred thousan’?” Jim asked, pausing and looking up from the unlacing of his shoe.
“Sure,” was the answer. “I seen a dance-house girl down in Arizona once, with some big sparklers on her. They wasn’t real. She said if they was she wouldn’t be dancin’. Said they’d be worth all of fifty thousan’, an’ she didn’t have a dozen of ’em all told.”
“Who’d work for a livin’?” Jim triumphantly demanded. “Pick an’ shovel work!” he sneered. “Work like a dog all my life, an’ save all my wages, an’ I wouldn’t have half as much as we got tonight.”

===Chinese===
“你真觉得它们值十万？”吉姆问，解鞋带的手停了，抬起头来。
“那可不，”回答是这么一句。“有一年我在亚利桑那见过一个舞厅姑娘，身上挂着几颗大亮货。不是真的。她说要是真的，她就不在这儿跳舞了。说那些统共值五万块，她满打满算还不到一打。”
“那谁还做工混饭吃？”吉姆得意洋洋地质问。“抡镐挥锨的活儿！”他嗤之以鼻。“像狗一样干一辈子，工钱一个子儿不花全攒下，也攒不到咱们今晚这一半。”

===Original===
“Dish washin’s about your measure, an’ you couldn’t get more’n twenty a month an’ board. Your figgers is ’way off, but your point is well taken. Let them that likes it, work. I rode range for thirty a month when I was young an’ foolish. Well, I’m older, an’ I ain’t ridin’ range.”
He got into bed on one side. Jim put out the light and followed him in on the other side.
“How’s your arm feel?” Jim queried amiably.

===Chinese===
“洗碗碟倒是你的尺寸，一个月外加食宿撑死二十块。你这笔账离谱得很，道理倒是站得住。谁爱做工谁做去。我年轻不懂事的时候，放马巡牧，一个月才三十。喏，如今我老了，也不巡什么牧了。”
他上了床，睡一侧。吉姆熄了灯，从另一侧跟着上了床。
“胳膊觉得怎么样？”吉姆和颜悦色地问。

===Original===
Such concern was unusual, and Matt noted it, and replied﻿—
“I guess there’s no danger of hydrophoby. What made you ask?”
Jim felt in himself a guilty stir, and under his breath he cursed the other’s way of asking disagreeable questions; but aloud he answered﻿—
“Nothin’, only you seemed scared of it at first. What are you goin’ to do with your share, Matt?”

===Chinese===
这份关切不同寻常，马特记下了，答道——
“我寻思狂犬病是没什么好担心的。你怎么想起问这个？”
吉姆心里有鬼，动了一下，暗地里咒骂对方这张专问扎心话的嘴；可嘴上答的是——
“没什么，就是起头你好像挺怕它。你那份打算怎么用，马特？”

===Original===
“Buy a cattle ranch in Arizona an’ set down an’ pay other men to ride range for me. There’s some several I’d like to see askin’ a job from me, damn them! An’ now you shut your face, Jim. It’ll be some time before I buy that ranch. Just now I’m goin’ to sleep.”
But Jim lay long awake, nervous and twitching, rolling about restlessly and rolling himself wide awake every time he dozed. The diamonds still blazed under his eyelids, and the fire of them hurt. Matt, in spite of his heavy nature, slept lightly, like a wild animal alert in its sleep; and Jim noticed, every time he moved, that his partner’s body moved sufficiently to show that it had received the impression and that it was trembling on the verge of awakening. For that matter, Jim did not know whether or not, frequently, the other was awake. Once, quietly, betokening complete consciousness, Matt said to him: “Aw, go to sleep, Jim. Don’t worry about them jools. They’ll keep.” And Jim had thought that at that particular moment Matt had been surely asleep.
In the late morning Matt was awake with Jim’s first movement, and thereafter he awoke and dozed with him until midday, when they got up together and began dressing.

===Chinese===
“到亚利桑那买座牧牛场，坐着花钱雇别人替我巡牧。有几位爷，我倒想瞧瞧他们上我门来讨活计的样子，去他们的！好了，闭上你的嘴，吉姆。牧场还得些日子才买得上。眼下我要睡了。”
可吉姆久久睡不着，神经紧张，肌肉直跳，翻来覆去，每每一合眼又把自己翻醒。钻石还在他眼皮底下烧，烧得发疼。马特别看性子滞重，睡得却浅，像睡里也竖着耳朵的野兽；吉姆每回一动就发现，同伙的身子都会相应地动一动，表明那印象已经收到，正悬在醒来的边上。说起来，那一位醒没醒着，吉姆常常根本拿不准。有一回，马特静静地——静得说明他完全清醒——对他开了口：“嗐，睡你的，吉姆。别惦记那些珠宝。跑不了。”而吉姆原先还当那一刻的马特准是睡熟了的。
上午过了一半，吉姆一动，马特就醒了；此后吉姆醒一回瞌睡一回，他也陪着醒一回瞌睡一回，直到晌午，两人一齐起床穿衣。

===Original===
“I’m goin’ out to get a paper an’ some bread,” Matt said. “You boil the coffee.”
As Jim listened, unconsciously his gaze left Matt’s face and roved to the pillow, beneath which was the bundle wrapped in the bandana handkerchief. On the instant Matt’s face became like a wild beast’s.
“Look here, Jim,” he snarled. “You’ve got to play square. If you do me dirt, I’ll fix you. Understand? I’d eat you, Jim. You know that. I’d bite right into your throat an’ eat you like that much beefsteak.”

===Chinese===
“我出去买张报、买点面包，”马特说。“你来煮咖啡。”
吉姆听着，不知不觉目光离开了马特的脸，游到那只枕头上——枕头底下压着印花手帕裹的那一包。刹那间，马特的脸变成了野兽的脸。
“听着，吉姆，”他龇出牙低吼。“你得照规矩来。你要敢黑我，我就收拾你。懂吗？我会把你吃掉，吉姆。这你知道。我会一口咬进你喉咙，把你当块牛排那么吃掉。”

===Original===
His sunburned skin was black with the surge of blood in it, and his tobacco-stained teeth were exposed by the snarling lips. Jim shivered and involuntarily cowered. There was death in the man he looked at. Only the night before that black-faced man had killed another with his hands, and it had not hurt his sleep. And in his own heart Jim was aware of a sneaking guilt, of a train of thought that merited all that was threatened.
Matt passed out, leaving him still shivering. Then a hatred twisted his own face, and he softly hurled savage curses at the door. He remembered the jewels, and hastened to the bed, feeling under the pillow for the bandana bundle. He crushed it with his fingers to make certain that it still contained the diamonds. Assured that Matt had not carried them away, he looked toward the kerosene stove with a guilty start. Then he hurriedly lighted it, filled the coffeepot at the sink, and put it over the flame.
The coffee was boiling when Matt returned, and while the latter cut the bread and put a slice of butter on the table, Jim poured out the coffee. It was not until he sat down and had taken a few sips of the coffee, that Matt pulled out the morning paper from his pocket.

===Chinese===
他那张晒红的脸，血一涌，黑了下来，咆哮翻起的嘴唇露出烟渍斑斑的牙。吉姆一哆嗦，不由自主地缩了缩。他望着的这个人身上带着死。就在头天夜里，这黑脸汉子还亲手结果过一个人，连觉都不曾耽误。而吉姆自己心里明白，他揣着一桩见不得人的亏心，揣着一串念头，方才那些威胁，他一桩桩都受得起。
马特出去了，留下他还在哆嗦。随后一股恨扭歪了他自己的脸，他冲着门，低低地泼过去一串狠毒的咒骂。他记起珠宝，赶紧奔到床边，伸手到枕头底下摸那手帕包。他用手指捏了捏，确认钻石还在里头。确信马特没把它们带走之后，他心里亏虚地一惊，朝煤油炉瞟了一眼。接着他匆匆点着炉子，在水槽上灌满咖啡壶，坐上了火。
马特回来时咖啡正开着，他切面包、往桌上搁一块黄油，吉姆倒咖啡。直到坐下、呷了几口咖啡，马特才从口袋里掏出晨报。

===Original===
“We was way off,” he said. “I told you I didn’t dast figger out how fat it was. Look at that.”
He pointed to the headlines on the first page.
“**Swift nemesis on Bujannoff’s track**,” they read. “**murdered in his sleep after robbing his partner.**”

===Chinese===
“咱们算得离了谱，”他说。“我早说过，肥到什么地步我不敢算。瞧这个。”
他指着头版的标题。
“**天诛在后，紧追布扬诺夫**”，“**抢劫合伙人之后，于睡梦中被杀**。”

===Original===
“There you have it!” Matt cried. “He robbed his partner﻿—robbed him like a dirty thief.”
“Half a million of jewels missin’,” Jim read aloud. He put the paper down and stared at Matt.
“That’s what I told you,” the latter said. “What in hell do we know about jools? Half a million!﻿—an’ the best I could figger it was a hundred thousan’. Go on an’ read the rest of it.”
They read on silently, their heads side by side, the untouched coffee growing cold; and ever and anon one or the other burst forth with some salient printed fact.
“I’d like to seen Metzner’s face when he opened the safe at the store this mornin’,” Jim gloated.
“He hit the high places right away for Bujannoff’s house,” Matt explained. “Go on an’ read.”

===Chinese===
“这不结了！”马特叫道。“他劫了他的合伙人——像个下三滥的贼一样劫了他。”
“价值五十万的珠宝失踪，”吉姆念出声。他放下报纸，瞪着马特。
“我早说了，”那一位说。“咱们懂个屁的珠宝。五十万！——我顶破天算出个十万。往下念，念完。”
两人头挨着头，默默往下读，没动的咖啡渐渐凉了；时不时这一个或那一个，突然冒出报上某条扎眼的事实。
“今儿早上梅茨纳打开铺子里那口保险柜时的脸色，我真想瞧瞧，”吉姆幸灾乐祸。
“他一准儿撒腿直奔布扬诺夫家去了，”马特解释。“接着念。”

===Original===
“Was to have sailed last night at ten on the *Sajoda* for the South Seas﻿—steamship delayed by extra freight﻿—”
“That’s why we caught ’m in bed,” Matt interrupted. “It was just luck﻿—like pickin’ a fifty-to-one winner.”
“*Sajoda* sailed at six this mornin’﻿—”
“He didn’t catch her,” Matt said. “I saw his alarm-clock was set at five. That’d given ’m plenty of time﻿ ﻿… only I come along an’ put the kibosh on his time. Go on.”
“Adolph Metzner in despair﻿—the famous Haythorne pearl necklace﻿—magnificently assorted pearls﻿—valued by experts at from fifty to seventy thousan’ dollars.”
Jim broke off to swear vilely and solemnly, concluding with, “Those damn oyster-eggs worth all that money!”

===Chinese===
“原定昨夜十时乘*“萨约达”号*启程赴南洋诸岛——轮船因临时加货延误——”
“所以咱们才在床上堵着他，”马特插话。“纯属手气——跟押中一赔五十的头马一个样。”
“*“萨约达”号*今晨六时启了航——”
“他没赶上，”马特说。“我瞧见他闹钟拨在五点。本来时间富余得很……偏偏我到了，把他的日程一笔勾销。接着念。”
“阿道夫·梅茨纳万念俱灰——著名的海索恩珍珠项链——粒粒珠光精选——专家估值五万至七万元。”
吉姆念不下去，破口大骂，骂得又毒又郑重，末了来一句：“这些该死的蚝蛋，值这么多钱！”

===Original===
He licked his lips and added, “They was beauties an’ no mistake.”
“Big Brazilian gem,” he read on. “Eighty thousan’ dollars﻿—many valuable gems of the first water﻿—several thousan’ small diamonds well worth forty thousan’.”
“What you don’t know about jools is worth knowin’,” Matt smiled good-humouredly.
“Theory of the sleuths,” Jim read. “Thieves must have known﻿—cleverly kept watch on Bujannoff’s actions﻿—must have learned his plan and trailed him to his house with the fruits of his robbery﻿—”

===Chinese===
他咂了咂嘴唇，又添一句：“那可是真美人儿，错不了。”
“巴西大宝石，”他接着念。“八万元——多枚第一等水的贵重宝石——数千粒小钻石，足值四万。”
“你不懂珠宝——不懂的这些还真值钱，”马特笑着说，脾气好得很。
“探员们的推断，”吉姆念道。“窃贼想必早有眼线——巧妙监视布扬诺夫的一举一动——想必已探知他的计划，尾随他携赃回了家——”

===Original===
“Clever﻿—hell!” Matt broke out. “That’s the way reputations is made﻿ ﻿… in the noospapers. How’d we know he was robbin’ his pardner?”
“Anyway, we’ve got the goods,” Jim grinned. “Let’s look at ’em again.”
He assured himself that the door was locked and bolted, while Matt brought out the bundle in the bandana and opened it on the table.

===Chinese===
“巧妙——个屁！”马特炸了。“名声就是这么挣来的……全靠报纸。咱们哪知道他黑的是自家合伙人？”
“管他呢，货在咱们手里，”吉姆咧嘴一笑。“再开开眼吧。”
他确认了门已上锁插闩，马特则取出手帕包，在桌上打开。

===Original===
“Ain’t they beauties, though!” Jim exclaimed at sight of the pearls; and for a time he had eyes only for them. “Accordin’ to the experts, worth from fifty to seventy thousan’ dollars.”
“An’ women like them things,” Matt commented. “An’ they’ll do everything to get ’em﻿—sell themselves, commit murder, anything.”
“Just like you an’ me.”
“Not on your life,” Matt retorted. “I’ll commit murder for ’em, but not for their own sakes, but for sake of what they’ll get me. That’s the difference. Women want the jools for themselves, an’ I want the jools for the women an’ such things they’ll get me.”
“Lucky that men an’ women don’t want the same things,” Jim remarked.
“That’s what makes commerce,” Matt agreed; “people wantin’ different things.”

===Chinese===
“这不是美人儿是什么！”一见到那串珍珠，吉姆就嚷开了；有一阵子他眼里再装不下别的。“照专家的说法，值五万到七万。”
“女人就爱这些东西，”马特评点。“为了弄到手，她们什么都干得出来——卖身、杀人，什么都干。”
“跟你我一样。”
“差得远，”马特回敬。“为它们我照样杀人，可不为它们本身，为的是它们能换来什么。分别就在这儿。女人要珠宝是为自己，我要珠宝是为了女人，为了它们能给我换来的一切。”
“幸亏男人跟女人要的不是一路东西，”吉姆说。
“买卖就是这么来的，”马特深以为然；“就靠人人要的东西不一样。”

===Original===
In the middle of the afternoon Jim went out to buy food. While he was gone, Matt cleared the table of the jewels, wrapping them up as before and putting them under the pillow. Then he lighted the kerosene stove and started to boil water for coffee. A few minutes later, Jim returned.
“Most surprising,” he remarked. “Streets, an’ stores, an’ people just like they always was. Nothin’ changed. An’ me walking along through it all a millionaire. Nobody looked at me an’ guessed it.”
Matt grunted unsympathetically. He had little comprehension of the lighter whims and fancies of his partner’s imagination.

===Chinese===
下半晌，吉姆出门买吃食。趁他不在，马特把桌上的珠宝收了，照旧包好，塞回枕头底下。然后点着煤油炉，烧水预备煮咖啡。几分钟后，吉姆回来了。
“真是不可思议，”他说。“街道、铺子、人，全跟平时一个样。什么都没变。我这么个百万富翁，打它们中间穿过去。竟没一个人看我一眼，猜出我的身份。”
马特哼了一声，毫无同感。他那位同伙想象里这点轻飘飘的兴致和花头，他领会不了。

===Original===
“Did you get a porterhouse?” he demanded.
“Sure, an’ an inch thick. It’s a peach. Look at it.”
He unwrapped the steak and held it up for the other’s inspection. Then he made the coffee and set the table, while Matt fried the steak.

===Chinese===
“上等牛排买了没有？”他问。
“买了，一寸厚。上品。你瞧。”
他解开牛排的包裹，举起来给对方验看。然后他煮咖啡、摆桌，马特煎牛排。

===Original===
“Don’t put on too much of them red peppers,” Jim warned. “I ain’t used to your Mexican cookin’. You always season too hot.”
Matt grunted a laugh and went on with his cooking. Jim poured out the coffee, but first, into the nicked china cup, he emptied a powder he had carried in his vest pocket wrapped in a rice-paper. He had turned his back for the moment on his partner, but he did not dare to glance around at him. Matt placed a newspaper on the table, and on the newspaper set the hot frying-pan. He cut the steak in half, and served Jim and himself.
“Eat her while she’s hot,” he counselled, and with knife and fork set the example.

===Chinese===
“那些红辣椒别搁太多，”吉姆告诫。“你这墨西哥做法我吃不惯。你总把口味弄得太冲。”
马特哼声笑了，接着做他的菜。吉姆倒咖啡，不过倒之前，先往那只缺了口的花瓷杯里，倒进一包药粉——那粉原用一张糯米纸裹着，揣在他马甲口袋里。这会儿他是背对着同伙的，可他连回头看一眼都不敢。马特往桌上铺了张报纸，把烧热的煎锅搁在报纸上。他把牛排切成两半，一份给吉姆，一份给自己。
“趁热吃，”他劝了一句，刀叉并举，先做了个样子。

===Original===
“She’s a dandy,” was Jim’s judgment, after his first mouthful. “But I tell you one thing straight. I’m never goin’ to visit you on that Arizona ranch, so you needn’t ask me.”
“What’s the matter now?” Matt asked.
“Hell’s the matter,” was the answer. “The Mexican cookin’ on your ranch’d be too much for me. If I’ve got hell a-comin’ in the next life, I’m not goin’ to torment my insides in this one. Damned peppers!”

===Chinese===
“好一块好肉，”头一口下肚，吉姆下了判词。“可有句话我把你说明白。你那亚利桑那牧场，我永远不去，死了这条心吧。”
“这又是怎么了？”马特问。
“地狱了呗，”回答是这么一句。“你牧场上的墨西哥菜我消受不起。来世反正有地狱等着我，这一世就别再糟蹋我这副五脏了。该死的辣椒！”

===Original===
He smiled, expelled his breath forcibly to cool his burning mouth, drank some coffee, and went on eating the steak.
“What do you think about the next life anyway, Matt?” he asked a little later, while secretly he wondered why the other had not yet touched his coffee.
“Ain’t no next life,” Matt answered, pausing from the steak to take his first sip of coffee. “Nor heaven nor hell, nor nothin’. You get all that’s comin’ right here in this life.”

===Chinese===
他笑了笑，用力往外呼气，给烧得慌的嘴降温，喝了口咖啡，接着吃牛排。
“说起来，你怎么看下辈子，马特？”过了一会他问，暗地里正纳闷对方怎么还一口咖啡没沾。
“没有什么下辈子，”马特答道，停下牛排，呷了头一口咖啡。“也没有天堂，没有地狱，什么都没有。该你得的一切，这辈子就全得了。”

===Original===
“An’ afterward?” Jim queried out of his morbid curiosity, for he knew that he looked upon a man that was soon to die. “An’ afterward?” he repeated.
“Did you ever see a man two weeks dead?” the other asked.
Jim shook his head.
“Well, I have. He was like this beefsteak you an’ me is eatin’. It was once steer cavortin’ over the landscape. But now it’s just meat. That’s all, just meat. An’ that’s what you an’ me an’ all people come to﻿—meat.”
Matt gulped down the whole cup of coffee, and refilled the cup.
“Are you scared to die?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“那往后呢？”吉姆问，出于一种病态的好奇，因为他知道，自己望着的是一个很快就要死的人。“往后呢？”他又问了一遍。
“你见过死了两个礼拜的人吗？”对方问。
吉姆摇了摇头。
“我可见过。他跟你我正吃着的这块牛排一个样。它从前是一头满野地撒欢的小公牛。可如今只剩肉了。就这么回事，光剩肉。你我、还有所有的人，到头来也是这个——肉。”
马特把一整杯咖啡一口灌了下去，又斟满一杯。
“你怕死吗？”他问。

===Original===
Jim shook his head. “What’s the use? I don’t die anyway. I pass on an’ live again﻿—”
“To go stealin’, an’ lyin’ an’ snivellin’ through another life, an’ go on that way forever an’ ever an’ ever?” Matt sneered.
“Maybe I’ll improve,” Jim suggested hopefully. “Maybe stealin’ won’t be necessary in the life to come.”
He ceased abruptly, and stared straight before him, a frightened expression on his face.
“What’s the matter!” Matt demanded.
“Nothin’. I was just wonderin’ ”﻿—Jim returned to himself with an effort﻿—“about this dyin’, that was all.”

===Chinese===
吉姆摇了摇头。“怕有什么用？再说我根本不会死。我不过是过去，然后再活——”
“再活过去接着偷，接着撒谎、哭鼻子，一辈子又一辈子里这么没完没了？”马特嗤笑。
“没准儿我会改好，”吉姆还抱着指望。“没准儿下辈子里，偷就用不着了。”
他猛地住了口，直勾勾望着前面，一脸惊惶。
“怎么了！”马特问。
“没什么。我就是瞎琢磨——”吉姆费力地把自己拽回来——“琢磨这个死，没别的。”

===Original===
But he could not shake off the fright that had startled him. It was as if an unseen thing of gloom had passed him by, casting upon him the intangible shadow of its presence. He was aware of a feeling of foreboding. Something ominous was about to happen. Calamity hovered in the air. He gazed fixedly across the table at the other man. He could not understand. Was it that he had blundered and poisoned himself? No, Matt had the nicked cup, and he had certainly put the poison in the nicked cup.
It was all his own imagination, was his next thought. It had played him tricks before. Fool! Of course it was. Of course something was about to happen, but it was about to happen to Matt. Had not Matt drunk the whole cup of coffee?
Jim brightened up and finished his steak, sopping bread in the gravy when the meat was gone.

===Chinese===
可那阵惊了他的恐怖，他甩不掉。仿佛一件看不见的阴惨之物打他身边经过，把它无形的影子罩在了他身上。他觉出一种不祥的预感。有什么大祸临头的事就要发生。灾殃悬在半空。他隔着桌子死死盯住对面那个人。他弄不明白。莫不是他失了手，毒了自己？不对，缺口杯在马特手里，他分明把药下在了缺口杯里。
下一个念头是：全是他自己的胡思乱想。这念头从前就捉弄过他。蠢货！当然是这样。当然有事要发生，可要发生在马特身上。马特不是把整整一杯咖啡都喝了吗？
吉姆打起了精神，吃完牛排，肉尽了还拿面包蘸着肉汁擦盘子。

===Original===
“When I was a kid﻿—” he began, but broke off abruptly.
Again the unseen thing of gloom had fluttered, and his being was vibrant with premonition of impending misfortune. He felt a disruptive influence at work in the flesh of him, and in all his muscles there was a seeming that they were about to begin to twitch. He sat back suddenly, and as suddenly leaned forward with his elbows on the table. A tremor ran dimly through the muscles of his body. It was like the first rustling of leaves before the oncoming of wind. He clenched his teeth. It came again, a spasmodic tensing of his muscles. He knew panic at the revolt within his being. His muscles no longer recognized his mastery over them. Again they spasmodically tensed, despite the will of him, for he had willed that they should not tense. This was revolution within himself, this was anarchy; and the terror of impotence rushed up in him as his flesh gripped and seemed to seize him in a clutch, chills running up and down his back and sweat starting on his brow. He glanced about the room, and all the details of it smote him with a strange sense of familiarity. It was as though he had just returned from a long journey. He looked across the table at his partner. Matt was watching him and smiling. An expression of horror spread over Jim’s face.
“My God, Matt!” he screamed. “You ain’t doped me?”

===Chinese===
“我小时候——”他起了个头，又猛地刹住了。
那件看不见的阴惨之物又扑了一下，他整个生命都为大祸将临的预感震颤起来。他觉出有一股分裂的力量正在他的皮肉里发作，全身的肌肉都有一种马上要抽动起来的先兆。他猛地靠回椅背，又猛地向前扑去，胳膊肘撑上桌面。一阵战栗幽幽地窜过他全身的肌肉。像风来之前叶子最初的那阵沙沙声。他咬紧了牙。它又来了，肌肉一阵痉挛般的绷紧。身体内部的这场哗变，让他尝到了恐慌。他的肌肉不再认他的管辖了。它们又一次痉挛着绷紧，不顾他的意志，因为他分明命令过它们不许绷紧。这是他身体里的革命，这是无政府状态；无能为力的恐怖猛地涌上他心头，他的皮肉箍住了他，仿佛一把攥住了他，寒气顺着脊梁爬上爬下，汗珠从额头上渗出来。他扫了一眼屋子，屋里的一切细节都带着一种奇怪的熟稔扑面而来，仿佛他刚从一趟长途旅行回来。他望过桌子去看他的同伙。马特正瞧着他笑。一片惊骇在吉姆脸上漫开。
“我的天，马特！”他尖叫起来。“你不会给我下了药吧？”

===Original===
Matt smiled and continued to watch him. In the paroxysm that followed, Jim did not become unconscious. His muscles tensed and twitched and knotted, hurting him and crushing him in their savage grip. And in the midst of it all, it came to him that Matt was acting queerly. He was travelling the same road. The smile had gone from his face, and there was on it an intent expression, as if he were listening to some inner tale of himself and trying to divine the message. Matt got up and walked across the room and back again, then sat down.
“You did this, Jim,” he said quietly.
“But I didn’t think you’d try to fix *me*,” Jim answered reproachfully.

===Chinese===
马特笑着，仍旧看他。接下来那阵发作里，吉姆没有昏死过去。他的肌肉绷紧、抽动、打成结，在那野性的钳制里弄疼他、挤压他。而在这一切当中，他忽然察觉马特举动不对劲。他走的也是同一条路。笑从他脸上没了，换成一副专注的神情，仿佛在听他自己内里的某个故事，想占出其中的讯息。马特起身，在屋里走了一个来回，又坐下。
“这是你干的，吉姆，”他静静地说。
“可我没想到你竟会来对付*我*，”吉姆答道，带着责备。

===Original===
“Oh, I fixed you all right,” Matt said, with teeth close together and shivering body. “What did you give me?”
“Strychnine.”
“Same as I gave you,” Matt volunteered. “It’s a hell of a mess, ain’t it?”
“You’re lyin’, Matt,” Jim pleaded. “You ain’t doped me, have you?”

===Chinese===
“嗐，我可把你办得妥妥的，”马特说，牙关咬得紧紧的，身子发着抖。“你给我下的是什么？”
“士的宁。”
“跟我给你下的一样，”马特主动交代。“这摊子烂透了，是不是？”
“你撒谎，马特，”吉姆哀告。“你没给我下药，对不对？”

===Original===
“I sure did, Jim; an’ I didn’t overdose you, neither. I cooked it in as neat as you please in your half the porterhouse.﻿—Hold on! Where’re you goin’?”
Jim had made a dash for the door, and was throwing back the bolts. Matt sprang in between and shoved him away.
“Drug store,” Jim panted. “Drug store.”
“No you don’t. You’ll stay right here. There ain’t goin’ to be any runnin’ out an’ makin’ a poison play on the street﻿—not with all them jools reposin’ under the pillow. Savve? Even if you didn’t die, you’d be in the hands of the police with a whole lot of explanations comin’. Emetics is the stuff for poison. I’m just as bad bit as you, an’ I’m goin’ to take a emetic. That’s all they’d give you at a drug store, anyway.”

===Chinese===
“下了，真下了，吉姆；也没有给你下过量。我把它做进了你那半块牛排里，做得那叫一个干净漂亮。——站住！你去哪儿？”
吉姆已经扑到门前，正在拉门闩。马特一蹿插到中间，把他搡开。
“药房，”吉姆喘着。“药房。”
“想都别想。你就乖乖待在这儿。休想跑出去到街上闹一出中毒的戏——枕头底下还压着那一堆珠宝呢。懂吗？就算你不死，也得落到警察手里，有大堆的账要交代。中了毒，催吐剂就是灵药。我中得跟你一样深，我也要催吐。反正到了药房，他们给你的也就是这个。”

===Original===
He thrust Jim back into the middle of the room and shot the bolts into place. As he went across the floor to the food shelf, he passed one hand over his brow and flung off the beaded sweat. It spattered audibly on the floor. Jim watched agonizedly as Matt got the mustard-can and a cup and ran for the sink. He stirred a cupful of mustard and water and drank it down. Jim had followed him and was reaching with trembling hands for the empty cup. Again Matt shoved him away. As he mixed a second cupful, he demanded﻿—
“D’you think one cup’ll do for me? You can wait till I’m done.”
Jim started to totter toward the door, but Matt checked him.

===Chinese===
他把吉姆搡回屋子当中，把门闩插死。他穿过地板往食品架走的时候，一只手抹过额头，甩掉成串的汗珠。汗珠摔在地板上，声音都听得见。吉姆眼睁睁地、万箭穿心地看着马特拿了芥末罐和一只杯子，奔向水槽。他搅了一杯芥末水，灌了下去。吉姆已经跟了过去，两只发抖的手伸向那只空杯。马特又把他搡开。他兑第二杯的时候，质问道——
“你以为一杯就够我用了？等着，等我弄完。”
吉姆晃晃悠悠又要往门口挪，被马特拦住了。

===Original===
“If you monkey with that door, I’ll twist your neck. Savve? You can take yours when I’m done. An’ if it saves you, I’ll twist your neck, anyway. You ain’t got no chance, nohow. I told you many times what you’d get if you did me dirt.”
“But you did me dirt, too,” Jim articulated with an effort.
Matt was drinking the second cupful, and did not answer. The sweat had got into Jim’s eyes, and he could scarcely see his way to the table, where he got a cup for himself. But Matt was mixing a third cupful, and, as before, thrust him away.

===Chinese===
“你再碰那扇门一下，我就拧断你的脖子。懂吗？等我弄完你再喝你的。就算它救得了你，我也照样拧断你的脖子。你横竖没有活路。我早跟你说过多少遍，黑了我是什么下场。”
“可你也黑了我，”吉姆费劲地把字咬出来。
马特正灌第二杯，没有作声。汗淌进了吉姆的眼睛，他几乎看不清去桌边的路——他给自己摸了只杯子。可马特又在兑第三杯了，而且同方才一样，把他搡开。

===Original===
“I told you to wait till I was done,” Matt growled. “Get outa my way.”
And Jim supported his twitching body by holding on to the sink, the while he yearned toward the yellowish concoction that stood for life. It was by sheer will that he stood and clung to the sink. His flesh strove to double him up and bring him to the floor. Matt drank the third cupful, and with difficulty managed to get to a chair and sit down. His first paroxysm was passing. The spasms that afflicted him were dying away. This good effect he ascribed to the mustard and water. He was safe, at any rate. He wiped the sweat from his face, and, in the interval of calm, found room for curiosity. He looked at his partner.
A spasm had shaken the mustard can out of Jim’s hands, and the contents were spilled upon the floor. He stooped to scoop some of the mustard into the cup, and the succeeding spasm doubled him upon the floor. Matt smiled.

===Chinese===
“说了让你等我弄完，”马特低吼。“别挡道。”
吉姆便扒住水槽，撑住他那抽动的身子，一面朝那杯代表活路的黄汤渴切地望着。他全凭意志才站着，才抓住水槽不放。他的皮肉拼命要把他对折起来、撂倒在地板上。马特喝下第三杯，艰难地挪到一把椅子跟前坐下。他的头一阵发作过去了。折磨他的痉挛正平息下去。这好处，他归功于芥末水。不管怎么说，他安全了。他抹掉脸上的汗，在平静的间歇里，竟还有心思好奇。他看了看他的同伙。
一阵痉挛把芥末罐从吉姆手里震落，里头的东西泼了一地。他弯腰去捧芥末进杯，紧跟着的一阵痉挛把他对折着撂倒在地板上。马特笑了。

===Original===
“Stay with it,” he encouraged. “It’s the stuff all right. It’s fixed me up.”
Jim heard him and turned toward him a stricken face, twisted with suffering and pleading. Spasm now followed spasm till he was in convulsions, rolling on the floor and yellowing his face and hair in the mustard.
Matt laughed hoarsely at the sight, but the laugh broke midway. A tremor had run through his body. A new paroxysm was beginning. He arose and staggered across to the sink, where, with probing forefinger, he vainly strove to assist the action of the emetic. In the end, he clung to the sink as Jim had clung, filled with the horror of going down to the floor.

===Chinese===
“接着喝，”他鼓励道。“就是这个灵。它把我救过来了。”
吉姆听见了，朝他转过一张被打垮的脸，扭曲着，满是苦楚和哀求。痉挛一阵接着一阵，直到他浑身抽搐，在地板上打滚，脸和头发在芥末里滚成了黄的。
马特瞧着这光景哑声笑起来，可笑到一半断了。一阵战栗窜过他全身。新的一轮发作开始了。他站起身，踉跄着挪到水槽边，用探进喉咙的手指，徒劳地想帮催吐剂一把力。到末了，他也同吉姆方才一样扒住了水槽，心里塞满了倒到地板上去的恐怖。

===Original===
The other’s paroxysm had passed, and he sat up, weak and fainting, too weak to rise, his forehead dripping, his lips flecked with a foam made yellow by the mustard in which he had rolled. He rubbed his eyes with his knuckles, and groans that were like whines came from his throat.
“What are you snifflin’ about?” Matt demanded out of his agony. “All you got to do is die. An’ when you die you’re dead.”
“I﻿ ﻿… ain’t﻿ ﻿… snifflin’﻿ ﻿… it’s﻿ ﻿… the﻿ ﻿… mustard﻿ ﻿… stingin’﻿ ﻿… my﻿ ﻿… eyes,” Jim panted with desperate slowness.

===Chinese===
对面那一位的发作过去了，他坐起来，虚弱、发晕，虚得站不起来，额头淌着水，嘴上挂着白沫——那沫子叫他在里头滚过的芥末染黄了。他用指节揉着眼睛，喉咙里发出一声声近乎呜咽的呻吟。
“你哭丧个什么劲？”马特从他的苦刑里挤出这句来。“你只消死就完了。死了，就是死了。”
“我……没……哭丧……是……芥末……辣……我的……眼睛，”吉姆拼死放慢了喘着说。

===Original===
It was his last successful attempt at speech. Thereafter he babbled incoherently, pawing the air with shaking arms till a fresh convulsion stretched him on the floor.
Matt struggled back to the chair, and, doubled up on it, with his arms clasped about his knees, he fought with his disintegrating flesh. He came out of the convulsion cool and weak. He looked to see how it went with the other, and saw him lying motionless.
He tried to soliloquize, to be facetious, to have his last grim laugh at life, but his lips made only incoherent sounds. The thought came to him that the emetic had failed, and that nothing remained but the drug store. He looked toward the door and drew himself to his feet. There he saved himself from falling by clutching the chair. Another paroxysm had begun. And in the midst of the paroxysm, with his body and all the parts of it flying apart and writhing and twisting back again into knots, he clung to the chair and shoved it before him across the floor. The last shreds of his will were leaving him when he gained the door. He turned the key and shot back one bolt. He fumbled for the second bolt, but failed. Then he leaned his weight against the door and slid down gently to the floor.

===Chinese===
这是他最后一回把话说成了。此后他只不成句地乱语，抖着两条胳膊在空中乱抓，直到新的一轮抽搐把他直挺挺放倒在地板上。
马特挣扎着挪回椅子，蜷在上面，双臂箍住膝盖，同他那正在溃散的皮肉搏斗。他从抽搐里出来时，浑身发凉，虚软。他望了望那一位的情形，看见他已经躺着不动了。
他想自言自语几句，想说说俏皮话，想对着人生发出他最后一声狞笑，可嘴唇只吐出些不成音节的响动。他想起一个念头：催吐剂失败了，剩下的出路只有药房。他望了望门，把自己拖起来站直。就在那儿，他一把抓住椅子，才没有摔倒。又一轮发作开始了。就在这阵发作当中，他的身体连同它的每一部分都在四散、翻扭、又绞回一个个死结，他扒住椅子，把它推在身前，一路蹭过地板。蹭到门口时，他意志的最后几缕正在离他而去。他拧开锁，拉开一道门闩。他摸第二道闩，没有摸着。于是他把全身的分量靠上房门，缓缓地滑倒在地板上。

## Created He Them / 造了他们

===Original===
She met him at the door.
“I did not think you would be so early.”
“It is half past eight.” He looked at his watch. “The train leaves at 9:12.”
He was very businesslike, until he saw her lips tremble as she abruptly turned and led the way.
“It’ll be all right, little woman,” he said soothingly. “Doctor Bodineau’s the man. He’ll pull him through, you’ll see.”

===Chinese===
她在门口迎住他。
“我没想到你会来得这么早。”
“八点半了。”他看了看表。“火车 9 点 12 分开。”
他原本一副公事公办的神情，直到看见她蓦地转身引路时嘴唇直打颤，才软了下来。
“会没事的，小妇人，”他宽慰道，“博迪诺大夫就是那号人物。他能把他拉回来的，你瞧着吧。”

===Original===
They entered the living-room. His glance quested apprehensively about, then turned to her.
“Where’s Al?”
She did not answer, but with a sudden impulse came close to him and stood motionless. She was a slender, dark-eyed woman, in whose face was stamped the strain and stress of living. But the fine lines and the haunted look in the eyes were not the handiwork of mere worry. He knew whose handiwork it was as he looked upon it, and she knew when she consulted her mirror.
“It’s no use, Mary,” he said. He put his hand on her shoulder. “We’ve tried everything. It’s a wretched business, I know, but what else can we do? You’ve failed. Doctor Bodineau’s all that’s left.”
“If I had another chance﻿ ﻿…” she began falteringly.

===Chinese===
他们进了客厅。他的目光不安地四下搜寻，随后转向她。
“阿尔在哪儿？”
她没有作答，却猛地一阵冲动，凑近他身边，一动不动地站住了。她是个瘦削的黑眼珠女人，脸上铭刻着生活的紧张与操劳。可那些细纹，还有眼睛里那股挥不去的惶惑，并不是寻常忧愁的手笔。他望着那张脸，知道那是谁的手笔；她照镜子的时候，心里也知道。
“没有用的，玛丽，”他说，手按在她肩上。“咱们什么法子都试过了。我知道这是桩揪心的事，可还有什么别的办法？你已经失败了。如今只剩博迪诺大夫这一条路了。”
“要是再有一次机会……”她颤声开了个头。

===Original===
“We’ve threshed that all out,” he answered harshly. “You’ve got to buck up, now. You know what conclusion we arrived at. You know you haven’t the ghost of a hope in another chance.”
She shook her head. “I know it. But it is terrible, the thought of his going away to fight it out alone.”
“He won’t be alone. There’s Doctor Bodineau. And besides, it’s a beautiful place.”
She remained silent.
“It is the only thing,” he said.

===Chinese===
“这话咱们早掰扯清楚了，”他厉声答道，“现在你得打起精神来。咱们得出的是什么结论，你清楚。再有一次机会你也毫无指望，这你也清楚。”
她摇了摇头。“我清楚。可一想到他要一个人出门，去孤身打完这一仗，实在太可怕了。”
“他不会孤单的。有博迪诺大夫呢。再说，那地方很美。”
她不作声。
“只有这一条路了，”他说。

===Original===
“It is the only thing,” she repeated mechanically.
He looked at his watch. “Where’s Al?”
“I’ll send him.”
When the door had closed behind her, he walked over to the window and looked out, drumming absently with his knuckles on the pane.
“Hello.”

===Chinese===
“只有这一条路了，”她机械地重复。
他看了看表。“阿尔在哪儿？”
“我去叫他。”
她出去，门在身后关上，他走到窗前朝外望着，指节漫不经心地敲着窗玻璃。
“你好。”

===Original===
He turned and responded to the greeting of the man who had just entered. There was a perceptible drag to the man’s feet as he walked across toward the window and paused irresolutely halfway.
“I’ve changed my mind, George,” he announced hurriedly and nervously. “I’m not going.”
He plucked at his sleeve, shuffled with his feet, dropped his eyes, and with a strong effort raised them again to confront the other.
George regarded him silently, his nostrils distending and his lean fingers unconsciously crooking like an eagle’s talons about to clutch.
In line and feature, there was much of resemblance between the two men; and yet, in the strongest resemblances there was a radical difference. Theirs were the same black eyes, but those of the man at the window were sharp and straight looking, while those of the man in the middle of the room were cloudy and furtive. He could not face the other’s gaze, and continually and vainly struggled with himself to do so. The high cheek bones with the hollows beneath were the same, yet the texture of the hollows seemed different. The thin-lipped mouths were from the same mould, but George’s lips were firm and muscular, while Al’s were soft and loose﻿—the lips of an ascetic turned voluptuary. There was also a sag at the corners. His flesh hinted of grossness, especially so in the eagle-like aquiline nose that must once have been like the other’s, but that had lost the austerity the other’s still retained.

===Chinese===
他转过身，回应刚进屋那人的一声招呼。那人朝窗前走来，脚步明显拖沓，走到半路又迟疑地站住了。
“我改主意了，乔治，”他慌忙而紧张地宣布，“我不去了。”
他扯扯袖口，两脚蹭来蹭去，垂下眼去，又费了好大的劲才重新抬眼，去迎住对方的目光。
乔治默默地打量着他，鼻孔微微张开，瘦削的手指不知不觉地弯了起来，活像鹰隼行将攫取的利爪。
就轮廓和五官而论，这两人有太多相像之处；然而，在最深的相像里，却藏着根本的不同。两人是同样的黑眼睛，可窗边那人的目光锐利、直视，屋子当中这人的目光却浑浊、躲闪。他不敢迎住对方的注视，又一回又一回徒劳地逼着自己去迎。两人是同样高耸的颧骨和其下的凹陷，可凹陷处的质地却仿佛不同。两人同样生着薄唇，出自同一副模子，只是乔治的嘴唇坚实有力，阿尔的却绵软松弛——那是苦行者堕成酒色之徒的嘴唇。嘴角还耷拉着。他的皮肉透出一股粗浊之气，尤其是那只鹰钩鼻，当年必定生得同对方的一模一样，如今却丢掉了对方至今还留着的那份冷峻。

===Original===
Al fought for steadiness in the middle of the floor. The silence bothered him. He had a feeling that he was about to begin swaying back and forth. He moistened his lips with his tongue.
“I’m going to stay,” he said desperately.
He dropped his eyes and plucked again at his sleeve.
“And you are only twenty-six years old,” George said at last. “You poor, feeble old man.”
“Don’t be so sure of that,” Al retorted, with a flash of belligerence.

===Chinese===
阿尔在屋子中央竭力稳住自己。沉默让他难受。他觉得自己眼看就要前后摇晃起来。他伸出舌头润了润嘴唇。
“我不走了，”他豁出去似的说。
他垂下眼睛，又扯起袖口来。
“你才二十六岁啊，”乔治终于开口，“你这个可怜的、衰弱的老人家。”
“这你可别太肯定，”阿尔反唇相讥，眼底闪过一丝好斗的光。

===Original===
“Do you remember when we swam that mile and a half across the channel?”
“Well, and what of it?” A sullen expression was creeping across Al’s face.
“And do you remember when we boxed in the barn after school?”
“I could take all you gave me.”
“All I gave you!” George’s voice rose momentarily to a higher pitch. “You licked me four afternoons out of five. You were twice as strong as I﻿—three times as strong. And now I’d be afraid to land on you with a sofa cushion; you’d crumple up like a last year’s leaf. You’d die, you poor, miserable old man.”

===Chinese===
“你还记得咱们横渡海峡那一英里半的事吗？”
“记得，那又怎么样？”阿尔脸上慢慢爬上一层乖戾。
“你还记得放学后咱们在谷仓里练拳的事吗？”
“你打我的那些拳，我全受得住。”
“我打你的那些拳！”乔治的嗓音陡然拔高，“一个星期五个下午，有四个下午是你赢。你那时抵我两个——抵我三个。如今我连拿沙发靠垫砸你都怕；你会像去年的枯叶一样一碰就碎。你会死的，你这个可怜的、凄惨的老人家。”

===Original===
“You needn’t abuse me just because I’ve changed my mind,” the other protested, the hint of a whine in his voice.
His wife entered, and he looked appealingly to her; but the man at the window strode suddenly up to him and burst out﻿—
